# ASP v0.2: what the spec needs from the build

11 additions to write into the spec as built, and 5 decisions — now resolved (2026-09-27). Numbers in brackets are the items' numbers in the earlier 40-item list.

The rest of what the build had to settle is in [implementation-notes.md](implementation-notes.md), with no decision needed. Unbuilt work is in [backlog.md](backlog.md), and single-player mocks are in [../MOCKS.md](../MOCKS.md).

## Decided (2026-09-27)

| # | Topic | Decision | What changed |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Learning data [32] | Metadata only, for now. No transcript content, diffs, or test output leaves the machine — only session counts, models, timestamps, tool-call counts. Revisit once the learning layer actually needs richer signals. | No code change; this is what's already built. |
| D2 | Probation after a runtime move [35] | Keep 7 days. Once a self-modification pathway exists, probation should force `self_modification` to `principal_approves` regardless of tier; nothing else is restricted. Who writes the canary suite stays open (Academy, Stage 2). | The registry now tracks each DID's current probation window (`EventLog.probation(did)`), derived from lineage `update` records carrying `probation_until`. Enforcement itself waits on a self-modification pathway, which doesn't exist yet (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| D3 | Identity bootstrap [20] | Keep trust-on-first-use for now. Must change before any hosted, multi-tenant network (already flagged in `docs/backlog.md`). | No code change. |
| D4 | Job diagram [13–16] | Adopt all three edges the build already added — two-step acceptance, one redelivery, revocation before delivery — into the spec's own diagram. They already match the spec's prose; only the diagram was narrower. | Spec-doc change (outside this repo); noted here for the record. |
| D5 | Log anchoring [21] | The log owner's own key periodically signs a checkpoint of the log's head; not published anywhere yet. | New: `asp log checkpoint --as <did>` signs `{seq, log_hash, signed_at}` and appends it to `~/.asp/checkpoints.ndjson`. `asp log verify` re-checks every stored checkpoint by independently replaying the log up to that seq (`EventLog.verifyCheckpoint`), not by trusting the stored value. |

## Write into the spec as built

| # | Spec section | Addition |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | Core objects: envelope [1, 2, 4] | Add `issued_at` and optional `cosigs`. `sig` is `{alg, kid, value}`. `id` = sha256 of the RFC 8785 canonical form of every field except id and signatures. Type strings look like `asp.mandate/v0.2`. |
| S2 | Core objects: numbers [3] | Signed records carry integers only; fractions are in permille. |
| S3 | Registry: identity [6] | v0.1 uses did:web for anyone who brings their own domain, and did:key (self-certifying, no domain) for anyone who doesn't; did:asp comes later without a breaking change. Added 2026-09-27, alongside the D1-D5 decisions, after the strategy review flagged that did:web-only identity ties "take your agent and leave" to whoever hosts the domain. |
| S4 | Core objects: record types [8, 9] | Passport, lineage edge, agent package, fleet and node become signed record types (17 in all, with Juror — see S12). Rebirth has none, by definition. |
| S5 | Bank: escrow [10] | The escrow lock travels inside the Bond record, since each transition emits one object. |
| S6 | Coordination: Contract [11] | The principal issues it, the performer co-signs it, and it is the first record of the job's chain. Intent/Offer (or Call/Proposal) are referenced, not chained. |
| S7 | Mandate [12] | Enumerate `self_modification`, `checkpoints` and `irreversible.policy`, and a dotted scope grammar such as `repo.read` and `pr.open`. |
| S8 | Registry: fleets [23] | A Fleet record is issued by its org. An agent joins by naming the fleet on its passport; it must share the fleet's sponsor, and the fleet must have room. |
| S9 | Registry: nodes [5, 24, 25] | A Node record delegates a short-lived key: it signs only as that node, never identity records, and expires. Under a Mandate, live nodes count against `max_parallel`. The person stays liable. A second Node record chained onto the first is an update — a rotation, or (setting `expires` at or before `issued_at`) an immediate revocation. |
| S10 | Transport: agent package [28, 29, 31] | Package layout, a manifest signed by the agent, and secrets as named placeholders that are never stored. May be a directory or a single gzipped tar file. |
| S11 | Learning: gates [35] | Memory changes and runtime moves are recorded as lineage `update` edges (layers `memory` and `backend`), written back after each successful run. A move's `probation_until` is tracked per DID in the registry. |
| S12 | Courts: juror stake (new, 2026-09-28) | A 17th record type, Juror (`spec/schemas/juror.schema.json`): self-issued, `{did, stake}`, chained per DID like Fleet/Passport — a later record updates the stake, locking more or returning some/all via the same credit ledger Bond/Settlement use. A ruling Attestation must be cosigned by a majority of the panel `drawPanel` draws (conflict-free: excludes the contract's principal, performer, and anyone they sponsor; seeded from the rejection that opened the dispute, so it's deterministic and replayable) — unless zero eligible jurors are registered anywhere, in which case any neutral DID may still rule, unchanged from the original mocked behavior (MOCKS.md #4). Chosen over a ledger-only (unsigned) stake specifically so staking is itself a real, verifiable, replayable commitment. |
| S13 | Bank/Registry: reputation as a slash consequence (new, 2026-09-28) | Deterrence, not a new record type: a DID's `tier`/`slash_count` (`EventLog.reputationOf`) is derived entirely from Settlement, never independently signed — same trust boundary as probation. A slash (`bond_slashed > 0`) demotes the backer's tier by one (floor 0) and counts it. `projectBond` then enforces two consequences on that same DID's *next* Bond: tier 0 is excluded from bonding outright (`tier_excluded`); below that, its own and its live fleet-mates' slash counts raise the minimum bond it must post for the contract's price (`bond_below_risk_floor` — 250‰ per own slash, 100‰ per fleet-mate's, capped at 1000‰). Agent-only: humans/orgs backing a job aren't tier-tracked. The 250‰/100‰ step sizes are a judgment call (like probation's 7 days), not a derived formula. |
| S14 | Courts: a ruling's fault is the settlement formula (new, 2026-09-28) | A ruling-backed Settlement (`basis: "ruling"`) can no longer declare arbitrary amounts: `projectSettlement` derives `escrow_released = floor(escrowLocked × (1000 − performerFault) / 1000)` and `bond_slashed = ceil(bondLocked × performerFault / 1000)` from the cited ruling's `fault` map, and rejects a Settlement that doesn't match (`settlement_mismatches_ruling`) — closing the gap where a bank could rule one way and settle another. `asp market settle` derives the same numbers automatically when the caller omits `--escrow-released`/`--bond-slashed`. Only applies when `basis: "ruling"`; `accepted` and `revoked` settlements are unaffected. |
| S15 | Learning: a slash shapes the agent's own next run (new, 2026-09-28) | No schema change — reuses the existing lineage `update` edge exactly as speced. `asp market settle` self-signs one for the slashed backer the moment `bond_slashed > 0` (only when that DID's own key is available locally; the log itself never signs on anyone's behalf). `asp pack` renders any such edges into `memory/PENALTIES.md`, which every runtime adapter's `materialize()` already copies into place with the rest of the package's memory — so the deterrent is literally in the agent's context at its next run, not just an entry in its signed history nobody reads. |
| S16 | Deterrence: tier 0 gates Mandate and Proposal too (new, 2026-09-28) | Extends S13's tier-0 exclusion past Bond: `EventLog.checkMandateTier` refuses a Mandate whose performer is at tier 0; `checkProposerTier` refuses a Proposal from a tier-0 issuer in allocation mode. Also: `asp orchestrate` now chains an immediate revocation onto a node's own grant the moment its task is known to have failed, instead of leaving the key live until its natural (up to 24h) expiry — the same chain-a-revocation pattern already used for manual node revocation. |
| S17 | Job lifecycle: principal-mode silence counts as acceptance (new, 2026-09-28) | A real lifecycle change, mirrored in both SDKs and covered by two new shared conformance vectors — unlike S12–S16, which were asp-log-only extensions on top of the lifecycle, not changes to it. `Contract` gets an optional `review_deadline` (mirrored from the cited Intent's `verification.review_deadline`, present only in principal-mode verification). `Settlement.basis` gets a fourth value, `"silence"`. A new lifecycle transition (`Delivered → Settled`, issuer `bank`, guard `past_review_deadline`: the settlement's own `issued_at` must be after the Contract's `review_deadline`) lets the bank close the job without ever citing an Acceptance Attestation — the principal's silence itself is never a signed record; what's signed is the bank's own settlement, timestamped after the deadline it's checked against. Vectors: `silence_after_deadline` (Settled), `settle_before_deadline` (`GUARD_FAILED`/`past_review_deadline` when settled too early). CLI: `asp market intent --review-deadline <iso>`, `asp market settle --basis silence`. |
| S18 | Subcontract nesting and Settlement fees (decided 2026-09-28) | Two of the four "needs a decision" items from that day, both decided as asp-log-only extensions (no lifecycle change, unlike S17). **Subcontract nesting**: an agent may hold the principal role on a Contract whose `parent_contract` names another — funded from its own balance, no automatic netting to the parent's escrow. `EventLog.checkSubcontract` requires the parent to exist, actually be a Contract, not already be Settled, and its `performer` to equal the child's `principal`. **Fees**: `Settlement.fees` now actually moves credits — deducted from the same escrow on top of `escrow_released` (checked against what was locked, like any other over-release), credited to a new local mock account, `EventLog.PLATFORM_DID`, standing in for a real platform/Insurer recipient (same trust boundary as the mocked bank/panel DIDs in conformance fixtures). CLI: `asp market contract --parent-contract <id>`, `asp market settle --fees <n>`. The other two items raised that day — appeals (to slash a juror's own stake) and the runtime→protocol compliance bridge — were deliberately deferred, each scoped as its own future slice rather than decided in passing. |
| S19 | Runtime → protocol compliance bridge: Action record (new, 2026-09-28) | An 18th record type, Action (`spec/schemas/action.schema.json`): self-issued, `{contract, scopes_used, summary?, artifacts?}`. It references a job's Contract by id without joining its own lifecycle chain — no lifecycle change, unlike S17. `EventLog.checkAction` requires the issuer to be that Contract's performer, the job to currently be Running or Checkpoint, and every `scopes_used` entry to already be granted by the Contract's live Mandate (tracked per-contract in a new `MandateRow`, projected from `mandate`, same pattern as `EscrowRow`); anything not granted is refused (`scope_violation`), and an unknown issuer, contract, or job state is refused outright. `artifacts` (optional, reusing the existing `artifactRef` common def) carries a hash of what a tool call's input actually was — data-flow fingerprinting — never the content itself, so a later dispute can prove what moved without the log ever holding it. Emission is real for one runtime as a proof-of-concept: `asp run --contract <id>` pipes the child process's live stdout and, for Claude Code, parses its existing `--output-format stream-json` tool_use blocks in real time via `LaunchPlan.checkOutputForAction`, mapping each call to a scope (`deriveScopeForTool`, shared with the existing pack-time `deriveScopes`) and a `sha256` fingerprint of its input. `deriveScopeForTool` also gained a `shell.network` split for `Bash`/`PowerShell` commands that look network-bound (`curl`, `wget`, `nc`, `ssh`, `scp`, a bare URL), narrowing what a blanket `shell.exec` grant quietly permits. CLI: `asp market action --contract <id> --by <did> --scopes-used <s> [--artifact <uri>=<sha256>]`. Honest limit, unchanged from the design discussion: self-reported, so a genuinely adversarial agent can simply not emit a violating action — this catches accidental scope creep and creates a real paper trail, not adversarial-proof monitoring. Codex/OpenHands emission and OS-level egress monitoring for fully-obfuscated indirect network calls are logged as backlog, not built (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S20 | Kill switch: a real-time consequence for a caught violation (new, 2026-09-28) | No new record type or lifecycle change — reuses S19's live Mandate check and the existing `basis: "revoked"` Settlement. Before spawning the runtime, `asp run --contract <id>` fetches the contract's live Mandate once (`EventLog.mandateOf`); as `checkOutputForAction` detects each tool call in real time, the first scope outside that Mandate stops the child process (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after a 3s grace period) instead of only being flagged once the run finishes. This can't undo the call that already happened — output is only ever seen after a tool call fires — but it stops every call after it, closing part of the "no live circuit-breaker" gap (`docs/backlog.md`). The economic consequence fires in the same motion: a `basis: "revoked"` Settlement, issued by the contract's own `bank` and cosigned by its `principal`, with `escrow_released: 0` and `bond_slashed` set to the full bond locked — the same split a Courts ruling of full fault would produce, without waiting for a human to notice the job stalled `Running` and open a dispute. Requires the bank's and principal's keys to be available locally to sign/cosign; without one, the kill still fires but the settlement is skipped with a logged note (an honest limit, same as the existing slash-lineage write in S15). Claude Code only, matching S19's own scoping. |
| S21 | Open-weight models on existing runtimes (new, 2026-10-04) | No record or lifecycle change. `asp run`/`orchestrate` take `--endpoint <url>` and `--api-key-env <NAME>` alongside `--model`; the OpenHands adapter sets `LLM_BASE_URL`/`LLM_API_KEY` and the Codex adapter generates an `asp_open` chat-completions provider override, so the same package runs on an open-weight model served from any OpenAI-compatible endpoint. The compliance bridge (S19) and kill switch (S20) extend to OpenHands via a `checkOutputForAction` that parses its headless ActionEvents. The parser follows the SDK's documented event shape and is tested against a fake only (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S22 | Pre-call Mandate hook: prevention, not just reaction (new, 2026-10) | No record or lifecycle change; builds on S19 and S20. Under `asp run --contract <id>` on Claude Code, the contract's live Mandate scopes are snapshotted at run start and a `PreToolUse` hook blocks (exit 2) any call whose scope, mapped exactly as the compliance bridge maps it, is not granted, before the call runs. The hook fails closed on any error, makes no network calls, and records each block locally (`blocked-calls.ndjson`, reported by `asp run`). The kill switch (S20) still fires on the attempted call and settles as before; the hook guarantees the call itself never executes, which removes S20's limit that it could only stop the next call. Verified on real Claude Code 2.1.268; OpenHands and Codex are not covered yet (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S23 | Strikes: a blocked attempt is recorded, not slashed (new, 2026-10) | Extends S22. The Action record (S19) gains an optional `blocked_attempts` array of `{scope, count}`: calls the runtime stopped before they ran because their scope wasn't in the Mandate. They are a signed strike, kept apart from `scopes_used` (which holds only what actually ran) and not checked against the Mandate by `checkAction`; a scope outside the Mandate in `scopes_used` is still refused. Policy in `asp run --contract` where the adapter blocks calls before they run: a blocked attempt does no harm, so it is recorded, not settled; a call that actually ran out of scope (the hook missing, failing open or timing out) stops the run with the full-fault settlement (S20); `--max-strikes N` (default 3) blocked attempts in one run read as probing and stop it the same way. `TodoWrite` and `ExitPlanMode` need no scope. Conformance vectors added: `valid_action_with_blocked_attempts`, `action_blocked_attempt_zero_count`, `action_blocked_attempt_missing_count`. Not yet: strikes do not raise the risk floor (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S24 | Outcome verification and evidence grades (new, 2026-10) | No new record type and no lifecycle change. `Delivery.result.claims` (optional) lists each claim with a declared evidence grade (`measured`, `simulated`, `predicted`, new common def `evidenceGrade`) and an optional evidence reference. `Contract` and `Intent.verification` gain an optional `verifier` DID. `Attestation` gains kind `verification` (a standalone record about a Delivery, like `allocation`) with verdict `confirmed`, `partly_confirmed` or `not_confirmed` and a per-claim `claims` list of `{index, grade}` where grade adds `unverified`. `EventLog.checkVerification` requires the issuer to be the contract's named verifier and not the principal, the performer, or anyone either sponsors; the job to be Delivered; one verification per Delivery; claim indices to exist and be unique; `confirmed` and `partly_confirmed` to grade every claim; and `confirmed` never to hide a downgrade. `checkVerifiedBeforeAcceptance` refuses an acceptance, and `checkVerifiedBeforeSilence` a silence settlement, for a contract that names a verifier unless the Delivery has a verification that is not `not_confirmed`; rejection is unaffected. A new `verifications` table (migration 008) projects the verdict per delivery. Eight conformance vectors added (schema count 56 to 64). CLI: `asp market intent|contract --verifier`, `asp market deliver --claim`, `asp market verify`. Delayed settlement with hold-back and clawback is a separate, later lifecycle change (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S25 | Approval gates: the Mandate says which scopes need the principal's say-so (new, 2026-10) | The Checkpoint record, the principal's `checkpoint_resolution` and the job lifecycle's Running/Checkpoint states already existed; this wires them to a live run. `Mandate.irreversible` gains an optional `scopes` list (granted scopes the policy applies to): `checkpoint` holds a call for approval, `forbid` blocks it, `allow` ungates it. `EventLog.checkMandateGates` refuses a Mandate that gates a scope it does not grant (`gate_not_granted`). In `asp run --contract` on Claude Code, the pre-call hook holds a gated call and asks `asp run`, which raises a performer-signed Checkpoint (`before_irreversible`, with the tool, scope and a 300-character summary) and waits for the principal's signed resolution: `approved` runs the call; `corrected` (with the reason), `picked`, or no answer within `--approval-wait` seconds (default 600) refuses it. A refusal is not a strike or a slash; the Checkpoint stays open after a timeout. CLI: `asp market mandate --gate`, `asp market checkpoint`, `asp market resolve`. Three conformance vectors added (schema count 64 to 67). The summary in the Checkpoint's `proposed_action` is masked for secret-looking text (`redactSecrets`) and cut to 300 characters before it reaches the log or the terminal. Known limits: masking is pattern-based, so an unusual secret shape could get through; only Claude Code holds calls; the log does not itself require an approved Checkpoint before an irreversible Action (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S26 | A call counts as having run only if the runtime says it did (new, 2026-10) | No record or lifecycle change; tightens S23 and S25. Before, an out-of-scope call whose result was not the hook's refusal was assumed to have run and settled with full fault, which misjudged a call that the runtime's own permissions refused (and an approved gated call refused afterwards). On Claude Code the Mandate hook script is also registered for `PostToolUse` and `PostToolUseFailure`, which fire only for a call that actually ran, and records each in `executed-calls.ndjson`; `asp run` judges an out-of-scope or gated call against that record, rechecked for 3 seconds. Ran (even if it failed): violation, full fault. Did not run: a strike (`strike ... refused by the runtime before it ran`), or for a gated call, not counted in `scopes_used`. Runtimes without such a record keep the old assumption (`LaunchPlan.executedCallsFile` unset). Limits: a record that is late or unwritable turns a real violation into a strike; the file lives in the run directory (`docs/backlog.md`). |
| S27 | Allocation panel check and passport-driven earnings_split (new, 2026-10) | The log rejects an `allocation` Attestation unless its issuer is in the cited Proposal's Call `panel` (`not_on_call_panel`; unknown Proposal/Call also rejected). The performer's passport `earnings_split.agent_permille` is snapshotted when the Bond is posted (escrows gains `agent_permille`, sql/009) and applied at Settlement: the performer keeps that share of `escrow_released`, the rest goes to its passport sponsor (`earnings_split_no_sponsor` if none). `Settlement.earnings_split` is optional and may only restate the snapshot (`earnings_split_mismatch`), so the bank cannot choose the split. CLI: `asp market settle --agent-permille <n>` (assertion only). |
| S28 | Strikes nudge the risk floor, and decay (new, 2026-10) | No schema change. Blocked attempts in an agent's Action records (S23) are kept as dated entries on its reputation row (`strike_log`, sql/010). At Bond time the risk floor gains 10‰ per strike from the last 30 days (counted back from the Bond's own `issued_at`, so replay is deterministic), the performer's plus, if different, the backer's, capped at 200‰, on top of the slash terms. Strikes never demote or slash and age out after 30 days. `reputationOf` returns `{tier, slashCount, strikes}` (strikes held, entries older than 30 days before the latest strike are dropped). Only agents are tracked. |
| S29 | Graduated tier limits (new, 2026-10) | No schema change. A Mandate's `spend.cap` and `spend.per_action_max` may not exceed what the performer's tier allows: tier 1 at most 100 credits, tier 2 at most 1000, tier 3 and above uncapped (tier 0 stays excluded, `tier_excluded`). Over the limit fails with `tier_limit_exceeded`. Humans and untracked DIDs have no limit. `nodes.max_parallel` is limited too: tier 1 at most 4, tier 2 at most 16, tier 3 and above unlimited (same `tier_limit_exceeded`). Limits are constants (`TIER_SPEND_LIMIT`, `TIER_PARALLEL_LIMIT` in log.ts), not yet configurable. |
| S30 | Compliance bridge on Codex (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change. `asp run --backend codex --contract <id>` parses `codex exec --json` events (command_execution, file_change, mcp_tool_call, web_search) and reports real scopes in the Action record. Shell commands are classified: inspection-only commands (cat, Get-Content, rg, git status/diff/log, ...) are `repo.read`; commands that change files (redirects, Set-Content, rm, ...) are `repo.write`; push/PR/test/network keep their scopes; anything else is `shell.exec`. Detection and stop only: no pre-call hook, so an out-of-scope call stops the run with the full-fault settlement and there are no strikes or approval gates. |
| S31 | OpenHands bookkeeping tools need no scope; Codex open-weight uses the Responses API (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change. OpenHands `finish`, `think` and `task_tracker` ActionEvents are not reported as scopes (a live run killed and fully slashed an agent for `tool.finish`). `asp run --backend codex --model --endpoint` sets the custom provider's `wire_api` to `responses`; current Codex rejects `chat`. |
| S32 | Checkpoint expiry (new, 2026-10) | Lifecycle change, mirrored in both SDKs and the vectors. `Checkpoint.expires` (optional timestamp). New transition: from `Checkpoint`, an `Attestation` of kind `checkpoint_resolution` with verdict `expired`, issued by the performer, guards `about_open_checkpoint` and `checkpoint_expired` (the record is issued at or after the Checkpoint's `expires`; a Checkpoint with no `expires` cannot be expired), returns the job to `Running`. The principal's own resolutions are unchanged. `asp run` sets `expires` to the approval wait and closes the Checkpoint itself when the wait passes (the gated call counts as refused, no strike, no slash); `asp market checkpoint --expires`, `asp market resolve --verdict expired --by <performer>`. If the run ends with a request more than 10 s from expiry, it is left open and a note says how to close it. Vectors: lifecycle 32 to 36, schema 67 to 70. |
| S33 | Panel fees (new, 2026-10) | No schema change; derived, never declared. On a `ruling` Settlement whose ruling was signed by jurors from the drawn panel, the log pays those signers 5% of the contract price (`PANEL_FEE_PERMILLE` = 50), split equally (remainder to the first). The loser pays: the performer's fault share comes from its bond left over, then from the slashed part (so the principal receives less of the slash), the rest from the principal's escrow left over; a side with too little is covered by the other, up to what is left. No fee when no juror is registered (the mocked-panel fallback), when the price is 0, or when no signer is on the panel. Open: a side with nothing left over pays nothing (for example a principal who loses after the whole price was released), and the rate is a constant. |
| S34 | did:key enforcement and did:web admission (new, 2026-10) | No schema or lifecycle change. (1) The log refuses a `did:key` passport (first or update) that does not carry the key the DID encodes (`did_key_mismatch`, `did_key_invalid`): the DID is the key, so nobody else can claim it. Deterministic and offline, so replay is unaffected. (2) `fetchDidWebKeys` / `passportKeysNotPublished` (asp-core `didweb.ts`) fetch a did:web document (https, or http for localhost only; no redirects, 64 KB and 5 s limits, `id` must equal the DID) and read its Ed25519 keys (multibase or OKP JWK). `asp identity export <did>` writes a passport; `asp identity register <file>` admits someone else's signed passport, for did:web only if the DID document publishes every key the passport declares (fail closed if it cannot be fetched), for did:key through the log, other methods only with `--trust-unverified` (loud). The fetch is an admission check by whoever runs the registry, never inside the log, so replay stays offline; a key a domain publishes later or withdraws is not re-checked. Verified with a real local HTTP server and a real HTTPS fetch of a public document (no public Ed25519 did:web was found to test against end to end). |
| S35 | Witnessed checkpoints (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change; operator tooling for log custody. `asp log export --out <file> [--since <seq>]` writes the records (with their original append times) plus the minted-credit totals a replica needs. `asp log import <file>` replays an export into a log, re-verifying every record, refusing a gap or a log that has diverged (`EventLog.importRecords`, `LocalLog.importRecords`). `asp log witness <export> --as <did>` makes a witness: it replays the export into its own replica (`<home>/replica`, separate from its identity log) and, only if the replay reproduces the exported head (seq and log hash), signs a checkpoint of it. The operator keeps those in `witnesses.ndjson` (`asp log witnesses add`); `asp log verify` re-checks each witness checkpoint against an independent replay, so a witness that saw a different history (a rewritten log, or a log shown to different people) fails, and `--min-witnesses n` requires n distinct witnesses (not the log owner; keys come from the log's passports or from a did:key). Limits: the log hash covers records only, so credits minted by `credits grant` (MOCKS.md #13) are carried in the export but are not vouched for; witnesses are independent only if they are, nothing publishes the checkpoints for you; a witness vouches for a prefix, so records after its seq are unwitnessed (verify reports how far). |
| S36 | Published checkpoint feeds (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change. `asp log publish --to <dir>` appends the home's signed checkpoints not yet published to `<dir>/feed.ndjson` (append-only, deduplicated by signature); `--with-export` also writes `<dir>/export.ndjson`, the whole log, only when asked (it makes the contents public). Nothing is uploaded: commit the folder to a public git repo or serve it statically. `asp log witnesses add <file | folder | URL>` reads a feed (a URL ending in `.ndjson`, or a folder URL meaning `feed.ndjson`); `fetchSmallText` (asp-core) refuses http except localhost, redirects, and over-size or slow answers; the fetch is tracked in `witness-feeds.json`, and re-adding a source fails if an entry published before has vanished (a rewritten feed). Verified with a real local HTTP server and a real HTTPS fetch from GitHub raw. Limit: append-only is checked only from the first time a source is read, and a host can still show different feeds to different readers (witnesses cross-checking each other is the answer, not built). |
| S37 | Courts fee reserve (new, 2026-10) | No schema change; derived. When at least one juror is registered at Bond time, each side (the escrow payer and the backer) also locks half the panel fee (`ceil(floor(price x 5%) / 2)`) at Bond, stored on the escrow row (`fee_reserve_principal`, `fee_reserve_backer`, sql/011). At a ruling Settlement the fee (S33) is paid from the loser's reserve first, then what it has left over, and the other side covers any shortfall; unused reserves return to their owners, and on every other settlement both reserves return in full. A Bond when jurors exist therefore needs the extra credits (`insufficient_balance` otherwise). No jurors, no reserve: the earlier behavior is unchanged. |
| S38 | Witnesses cross-check each other (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change. `findEquivocations` finds two checkpoints with the same signer and seq but different hashes: a signed proof that the signer showed different readers different histories. `asp log cross-check <file | folder | URL>...` compares everything this home holds with feeds others saw, and also flags a signed checkpoint this log's own replay contradicts; exit 1 with both entries printed. `asp log witnesses add` runs the same check and refuses a feed that contradicts what is held. `asp log publish --seen` shares the witness checkpoints a home collected, so readers can compare what they each saw. Limit: it detects a fork once two readers compare; it cannot stop one, and a reader who never compares sees nothing. |
| S39 | Fleet isolation (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change; tooling. `asp identity copy <agent> --count n` makes independently liable copies: each a fresh did:key agent with its own passport, key, ledger account and reputation, issued by the original's sponsor, starting at the original's CURRENT tier (a tier-0 agent cannot be copied, so copies cannot launder a record). `asp orchestrate --isolate` runs each node as its own copy and issues the node grant from the copy. Limit: a copy starts with no strikes, so strikes can be shed by copying; the sponsor-level cost (fleet slashes raising the next bond) is unchanged. |
| S40 | Whistleblower reports (new, 2026-10) | Protocol change (schema + log rules; no lifecycle change, reports are standalone Attestations like allocation and verification). New attestation kinds `report` (`about` = a contract, `reasons` required) and `report_ruling` (`about` = a report, `verdict` upheld or dismissed). A `report` may be filed by any DID with a passport that is not a party to the contract or sponsored by one, against a contract that is Running or at a Checkpoint with a live bond, one open report per contract; it locks a deposit equal to the panel fee (5% of the price). A `report_ruling` needs a majority of a panel drawn for that report (seed = the report, excluding the principal, performer, reporter and whoever they sponsor); with no juror registered a report cannot be ruled on. Upheld: the deposit returns, the accused's bond pays the panel fee and gives the reporter 20% of what is left, and the contract's Settlement must be full fault (`report_requires_full_fault`: nothing released, no fees, nothing returned, the remaining bond slashed); `asp market settle` defaults to exactly that. Dismissed: the deposit pays the jurors. Store: `reports` table, `escrows.forced_fault` (sql/012). CLI: `asp market report`, `report-rule`, `panel draw --report`. Vectors: schema 70 to 76. Limits: only running contracts; the stop itself is still the principal/bank settling (cohort stop is M4); jurors are paid from the accused or the reporter, not by anyone else. |
| S41 | Contagion watcher (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change; tooling. `asp watch [--min-agents n] [--window s] [--draft-by did] [--all]` scans Action records for a technique spreading between different agents: same-input (one tool-call input fingerprint from n or more agents inside the window, only counting Actions that used a risky scope: shell.exec, shell.network, repo.push, pr.merge, pr.open, mcp.*, tool.*) and same-probe (the same scope refused by the hook for n or more agents). Lists the contracts involved and whether each can still be reported; `--draft-by` prints the `asp market report` commands. Exit 1 when it finds something. `asp market action --blocked scope=count` reports blocked attempts by hand. Limit: it sees only what Actions carry (self-reported, Claude Code and Codex), and identical inputs can be innocent: a finding is a lead, not a verdict. |
| S42 | Cohort stop (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change; tooling on existing records. `asp market cohort-stop --report <upheld report>` finds the watcher clusters that include the reported contract and settles every running job in them with a `revoked` Settlement: the reported job with the full fault the report forces, the others with escrow back to the principal and the bond SLASHED by default (they were running the same flagged pattern; the principal and bank sign that choice, decided 2026-10-08), or the bond returned with `--spare`. Each job's bank and principal keys must be available locally. Already-settled jobs are left alone. |
| S43 | Evaluation harness core (new, 2026-10) | Tooling. `asp eval run [scenario.json] [--agents n] [--exploiters n] [--spare] [--out report.json]` runs a scenario through the real CLI commands against a fresh log in a temp folder, on a simulated clock (`setClock`), and reads the measures back from the log: seconds to the first report, exploiters before it, exploiters stopped and never stopped, honest agents stopped (false positives), bond slashed and returned, whistleblower and juror earnings, `EventLog.verify()`. One kind exists, `swarm-exploit` (scenarios/swarm-exploit.json); its panel is an oracle that knows who exploited. Default run (20 agents, 6 exploiters): first report 61 simulated seconds after the first exploit with 3 exploiters active, all 6 stopped, 0 honest agents stopped, 88 bond slashed and 0 returned (48 slashed and 40 returned with `--spare`). Limits: scripted agents only; real runtimes write the same records and can be analysed from their log, not yet driven by the harness; no model matrix yet. |
| S44 | Antigravity (agy) adapter (new, 2026-10) | No protocol change. A fourth runtime, `antigravity` (Google Antigravity CLI, `agy`, installed 1.3.1): `asp pack --runtime antigravity` captures AGENTS.md / GEMINI.md, `.agents/rules/*.md` and `.agents/skills` (with `--include-user` also the `~/.gemini` copies); `asp run --backend antigravity` runs `agy -p <instructions + task> --output-format stream-json [--model m] --print-timeout 15m` and writes nothing into the project or `~/.gemini`. **Compliance bridge** (checked live): events are `{event: step_update, step_update: {...}}`; a tool step is reported when its ACTIVE event arrives; the 58 tool names `agy` reports are mapped (`run_command` by what the command does, read, write, exec, web and browser tools to `repo.read`, `repo.write`, `shell.exec`, `web.read`, `browser.use`, `call_mcp_tool` to `mcp.<server>.<tool>`, bookkeeping tools none, anything else `tool.<name>` which fails safe); a result with status ERROR fails the run. **Pre-call prevention** (checked live): under a contract, `agy` runs in a scratch workspace in the run dir that holds `.agents/hooks.json` with PreToolUse and PostToolUse hooks, and the project is added with `--add-dir`, so nothing is written into the project. The hook (`antigravity-mandate-hook.mjs`) answers `deny` for an out-of-scope call (agy reports it as state ERROR with `denied by pre-tool hook`, which `asp run` counts as a strike), records executed calls from PostToolUse, supports `--gate` approvals through the same Checkpoint flow, and fails closed (a crashing hook also blocks the call). An `allow` does not widen agy's own permissions (headless agy still refuses shell commands that are not pre-approved; `ASP_ANTIGRAVITY_ARGS=--dangerously-skip-permissions` for a run you trust). Findings: detection alone was too late (the first live run wrote the file before the kill landed); `--mode plan` does not stop writes; `agy -p` does not hang when its output is piped (agy 1.3.1); hook commands run through `cmd`, so the script path must be unquoted (Windows folder names with spaces use the 8.3 short form); hooks need `node` on PATH. Env: `ASP_ANTIGRAVITY_BIN`, `ASP_ANTIGRAVITY_ARGS`, `ASP_ANTIGRAVITY_TIMEOUT`. Limits: subagents (`invoke_subagent`) are scoped as `tool.invoke_subagent`, so a Mandate must grant it, and whether their own calls reach the hook is unchecked; MCP servers, permission rules and sessions are not captured; project instructions are carried in the prompt under a contract because the project is only an added directory. |
| S45 | Antigravity follow-up checks (new, 2026-10) | Checked live on agy 1.3.1: **gates** work through the hook (a gated repo.write was held, raised as a Checkpoint, written only after the principal approved; refused with a reason, nothing written, no strike). **Subagents:** the pre-call hook DOES see a subagent's own calls and blocks them, even with agy's permission prompts bypassed (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`), but those calls never appear in the output stream, so `asp run` now merges the hook's own records into the Action: `LaunchPlan.blockedCallsFile` adds blocked calls not seen in the stream to `blocked_attempts` (the larger count per scope wins, so nothing is double counted) and the `scope` on each executed-calls line to `scopes_used`; spawning a subagent is scoped `tool.invoke_subagent`, so a Mandate must grant it. **MCP:** the call is `call_mcp_tool` with `ServerName`, `ToolName`, `Arguments`, mapped to `mcp.<server>.<tool>` (confirmed: blocked when not granted, reported when granted); headless agy also refuses MCP calls (and shell commands) that are not pre-approved in its own settings, so a granted scope still needs `--dangerously-skip-permissions` or an allow rule in agy settings. With the hook installed, passing `ASP_ANTIGRAVITY_ARGS=--dangerously-skip-permissions` makes the Mandate hook the only gate, which held in every live test. **Pro model:** `gemini-3.1-pro-low` ran a read-only multi-step task (11 tool calls, 3 recorded as executed by the hook, the rest errored or blocked): it tried a shell command and a subagent despite being told not to modify anything and both were blocked (2 strikes, nothing written); some of its own tool calls were rejected by agy as malformed. `agy` also offers Claude Sonnet and Opus 4.6 and GPT-OSS 120B (open weight) through the same sign-in. |
| S46 | OpenHands live fixes (new, 2026-10) | Checked on real OpenHands 1.16 (WSL) with an open-weight model, `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free` through OpenRouter, via `--model openai/<id> --endpoint https://openrouter.ai/api/v1 --api-key-env <NAME>`. The real `ActionEvent` shape matches the adapter: `{kind: ActionEvent, tool_name: terminal, action: {command, is_input, timeout, reset}}` and `finish` with `action.message`. Two bugs found and fixed: (1) a terminal command was scoped as `shell.exec` even when read-only, so a read-only job running `ls` or `cat` was killed and fully slashed; it is now scoped by what the command does (`scopeForShellCommand`, as for Codex and Antigravity). (2) `asp run --contract` ran the agent with no limits at all when the contract had no live Mandate (the agent had been demoted by the earlier slash, so its next job never reached Running); it now refuses to run unless the contract is Running or at a Checkpoint. Limit unchanged: OpenHands has no pre-call hook, so an out-of-scope call is detected and the run stopped after the call (the file in the live write test was still written). |
| S47 | Real agents in the swarm harness; runtime-independent shell fingerprint (new, 2026-10) | `asp eval run` scenarios gain `real`: agents on actual runtimes (claude-code, codex, antigravity, openhands), each its own independently liable copy with its own package and job, run through `asp run --contract` at `realFirstStep` and then every `realEvery` steps, told to run the exploit command (`exploiter: true`) or given an ordinary task; `--real backend[:model][:exploit|honest]` adds them from the command line; per-agent `env`, `endpoint` and `apiKeyEnv` (a key for an OpenAI-compatible endpoint is read from `~/.asp-openrouter-key` into the harness process only). What an agent really did is read back from the log (its Action carries the exploit's fingerprint), and the report lists each real agent: told to exploit, used it, stopped, exit code, seconds, notes. The panel upholds a report when the cluster it came from is the exploit's, so false positives are measurable. To make cross-runtime clusters possible every adapter now fingerprints a shell command as `asp://shell-command` plus the SHA-256 of the normalized command (wrapper removed, whitespace collapsed; `shellArtifact` in codex-actions.ts), the same on Claude Code (Bash/PowerShell), Codex (command_execution), Antigravity (run_command) and OpenHands (terminal); other tools keep `asp://tool-call/<name>`. `Io.raw` routes a child runtime's echoed output (the harness discards it). First live mixed run (scenarios/swarm-mixed.json): 6 scripted agents and 5 real ones. Claude Code (haiku-5-5), Codex and Antigravity (gemini-3.8-flash-low) each ran the exploit command; the watcher saw it across the three runtimes at the third agent, 61 simulated seconds after the first use; the report was upheld and the cohort stop settled all three real jobs (one full fault, two slashed). OpenHands (Nemotron 120B, OpenRouter free) used it afterwards and was reported separately; the scripted exploiters were then reported one by one; the fourth hit the report cap (`maxReports` 4) and was never stopped. The honest agent (Antigravity, GPT-OSS 120B) did not run: agy answered 503 no capacity (a transient server error, correctly shown as a failed run, not a false stop). 0 honest agents stopped, 88 bond slashed, log verified (114 records). Observation: every later exploiter needs its own report; an upheld fingerprint is not yet remembered (a known-bad fingerprint list for the hook is the obvious next step). |
| S48 | Log service (new, 2026-10) | `asp serve --db <postgres-url \| local:<dir>> (--tokens <file> \| --no-auth) [--port] [--host]`: one shared log behind HTTP, for many clients. POST /rpc takes `{target, method, args}` and calls an allowlisted `EventLog` method (reads, `verify`, `drawPanel`, ...), plus `append`, `importRecords` (any tenant) and `mint` (admin only). Tenants are bearer tokens stored as SHA-256 hashes (`asp serve token`, shown once); `--no-auth` is refused except on 127.0.0.1. Clients set `ASP_LOG_URL` (+ `ASP_LOG_TOKEN`) and every command then uses the shared log (`RemoteLog`); their keys stay on their machine and the service re-verifies every record, so a client cannot write what the log would refuse. Errors keep their ASP code across the wire. The `LogHandle` interface (LocalLog, RemoteLog, `postgresHandle`) is what the CLI talks to. Verified on the file backend and on Postgres (PGlite). Not yet: rate limits per tenant, TLS (put it behind a proxy), per-tenant log partitioning (one log, many tenants). |
| S49 | Checkpoint snapshots (new, 2026-10) | `snapshot.json` in a local log folder holds the sequence, the chain hash and the whole derived state; opening loads it, validates it against the hash chain recomputed from the ids in `log.ndjson`, and replays only the records after it (and any mints added since). `asp log snapshot` writes one; one is written automatically every `ASP_SNAPSHOT_EVERY` replayed records (default 1000, 0 turns it off). A snapshot that does not match the log is ignored (full replay). `asp log verify --full` replays from genesis and compares the result with the snapshot-loaded state. 1,000 records: open by full replay 1781 ms, from a snapshot 11 ms. The snapshot is a cache, never a source of truth. |
| S50 | Memory merge and budget (new, 2026-10) | What a run learns is merged three-way into the package's memory (`mergeMemoryInto`): the memory the run started from, the package's memory now, and the run's. Two runs of one agent no longer overwrite each other: new files from either are kept; a topic file both changed differently is kept side by side (`<file>.<label>.md`, with a note); an index file is the union of its lines; a removal applies only to a file nobody changed since. `asp orchestrate` consolidation uses the same merge. Memory is then held to a budget (`enforceMemoryBudget`): 200 topic files, 1 MiB, 200 index lines by default (`--memory-max-files`, `--memory-max-bytes`, `--memory-max-index-lines`); over budget, the least recently changed topic files are pruned first together with the index lines that point at them, and the lineage update says how many were pruned. Not yet: ranking by usefulness instead of age, summarising instead of pruning. |
| S51 | Per-tenant package storage (new, 2026-10) | `asp serve --packages <dir>` stores each tenant's agent packages under `<dir>/<tenant>/`. `PUT /packages/<name>` takes a `.aspkg.tgz`; the service unpacks and `verifyPackage`s it before storing (422 if it does not verify). The ETag is the SHA-256 of the stored bytes; replacing needs `If-Match: <etag>`, creating needs `If-None-Match: *`, anything else is 428, and a stale or existing copy is 412, so one copy never silently overwrites a newer one. Limits: 64 MiB per package, 256 MiB per tenant (a tenant entry may set `quotaBytes`; `asp serve token --quota-mb`). Tenants cannot see each other's packages; an admin can read another tenant's with `?tenant=` but not write them. CLI: `asp package push <package> --name <n>`, `pull <n> --out <dir> [--merge]`, `list`, `delete <n>`; the last etag and the memory both copies started from are kept in the ASP home. After a refused push, `pull --merge` merges this copy's memory into the service's (S50 merge), re-signs with the agent's key and the next push goes through. Limit: lineage edges recorded only on the local copy are not carried into the merged package, only the memory is. |
| S52 | The commons (new, 2026-10; a platform feature, not a protocol record) | Shared knowledge with citations and review, hosted by `asp serve --commons <dir>`. Entries, reviews and citations are small signed documents (`asp.commons/v0`); the service checks that the signing key is a current key of the DID the document speaks for in the log, and verifies the signature. An entry (id = hash of the signed document) has a title, text, up to 10 tags and optional contract; a review is `endorse` or `dispute`, one per reviewer, never by the author; a citation records that an agent used an entry, with a line of context, and counts once per citing agent, never the author's own. Status: `reviewed` after two other agents endorse and disputes are fewer than endorsements; `disputed` when disputes match or outnumber endorsements; else `unreviewed`. CLI: `asp commons add\|list\|show\|review\|cite`; `add --contract <id>` refuses unless that job's Mandate has `learning.share_to_commons` (the Mandate's own switch). Not yet: weighting reviews by the reviewer's reputation, stake or slashing for bad entries, takedown by a Court, injecting reviewed entries into a run's memory. |
| S53 | Live fixes from run D (new, 2026-10) | Found running S48-S52 live (real Postgres, two tenants, real Claude Code on two machines of one tenant at once, real Codex). (1) An agent writing its own memory is not a project change: the Write/Edit of a file inside the run's memory folder needs no scope, like the planning tools, in both the pre-call hook (`asp-mandate.json` now carries `memoryDir`) and the compliance bridge; every other path still maps to `repo.write`. Before this, a Claude Code agent under a `repo.read` Mandate saving a memory note was blocked and struck twice. (2) `asp run --prompt` closes the runtime's stdin: Codex also reads stdin when it is a pipe, and hung forever under a wrapper (five minutes, then killed) until this. (3) `asp market mandate --share-to-commons` sets `learning.share_to_commons` (it was always false), which `asp commons add --contract` checks. |
| S54 | Known-bad list (new, 2026-10; a platform list, not a protocol record) | After an upheld report, the command that spread can be listed so later agents cannot run it. `asp known-bad add --report <upheld> --by <did> [--fingerprint <asp://shell-command#sha256:...> | --all]` checks the report is upheld in the log and that the reported job really ran the command; without `--fingerprint` the candidates are the shell commands shared by three or more agents (the `asp watch` clusters) that include the reported job. The list is a file in the ASP home or, on a shared network, the log service's (`asp serve --known-bad <dir>`: any tenant reads, only an admin adds). `asp run --contract` gives it to the pre-call hook (Claude Code and Antigravity), which fingerprints each shell command the way the compliance bridge does (wrapper removed, whitespace collapsed, SHA-256) and blocks a match before it runs even when its scope is granted, with the Mandate-style reason `ASP Mandate: the known-bad list (an upheld report, <id>) marks this command harmful`. A block is a blocked attempt like any other: it counts as a strike and goes into the Action's `blocked_attempts`, and the Action no longer reports the blocked command as an artifact the agent used. Codex and OpenHands have no pre-call hook, so the list is not enforced there and the run says so. Limits: exact commands only (a changed command is a new fingerprint), shell commands only, no expiry or removal yet, a fingerprint is listed by a person with the report, not automatically. |
| S55 | Gateway P0 spike (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change yet) | `asp gateway --contract <id> --by <agent> (--openai-upstream <base url> | --anthropic-upstream <origin>) [--openai-key-env NAME] [--max-strikes n] [--token-cap n] [-- <command>]` runs any command under the contract's Mandate through a local proxy for the OpenAI-compatible chat API and the Anthropic Messages API (`packages/asp-package/src/gateway/`). The command gets `OPENAI_BASE_URL` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` pointing at the proxy. Every model reply is read for the tool calls it asks for; a call is mapped to an ASP scope (shell commands by what they do, with the same fingerprint as the hooks; read, write, web and MCP tool names; unknown tools become `tool.<name>`) and checked against the live Mandate and the known-bad list. A refused call is removed from the reply before the agent receives it (replaced by a short refusal note; the finish or stop reason follows) and counts as a blocked attempt; three in one run stop the gateway, as does the contract leaving Running or a token cap. The gateway can hold the provider key so the agent never sees it. A client that asked for a stream gets one built from the edited reply. When the command exits the gateway reports one Action (scopes used, blocked attempts, fingerprints, a summary line). Other paths pass through unjudged and are counted. Limits of P0: replies are fetched whole (no live streaming), no approval gates, no MCP proxy, no memory tools, no `assurance` field on the Action yet (needs schema and vectors), the Responses API (Codex) and Gemini are not judged, tool calls written as text by weaker models are not seen. Checked live with an open-weight model (Nemotron 120B through OpenRouter) driving a bare tool-calling loop that knows nothing about ASP: its `git push` call was removed before the loop saw it. |
| S56 | Gateway P1, and the Action's `assurance` field (new, 2026-10; protocol change for the field) | **Protocol:** the Action record gains an optional `assurance`: `self_reported`, `runtime_observed`, `gateway_observed`, `gateway_enforced`, `hook_enforced` or `sandbox_enforced`, the strongest mechanism that applied, so a principal or a Court can weigh the evidence (schema, two conformance vectors, `asp market action --assurance`; `asp run` writes `hook_enforced` when the adapter blocks calls before they run and `runtime_observed` otherwise; the gateway writes `gateway_enforced`). **Gateway:** (1) streams are relayed live: text and thinking flow through as they arrive, a tool call is held until it is complete, judged, then re-emitted whole or dropped, and blocks are renumbered so a dropped call leaves no gap (OpenAI-compatible chat and Anthropic Messages); (2) approval gates: calls on the Mandate's gated scopes are held and raised as the usual Checkpoint, answered by the principal's signed resolution (`approved` releases the call, `corrected`, `picked` or no answer refuses it); a forbidden scope is refused; neither is a strike; (3) a contract that is revoked, killed or settled mid-run stops the gateway, which refuses every further request (the log is re-read, since a local log is only read when opened); (4) Actions are reported while the run goes on (every 30 s, `ASP_GATEWAY_FLUSH_MS`) and at exit, because a contract that has ended can no longer take one; (5) `--approval-wait`. Checked live: real Claude Code (streaming, subscription login passed through) with no ASP hook installed, Mandate `repo.read`: two `curl` calls were refused before the agent received them, the Read ran, one Action recorded. Not yet: MCP proxy and memory tools (P2), Responses API and Gemini, tool calls written as text, the sandbox level. |
| S57 | Gateway P2: MCP, and memory and commons tools for any agent (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change) | The gateway serves MCP over Streamable HTTP at `/mcp/<name>` (JSON-RPC in POST bodies, JSON replies; no server-initiated stream). (1) **The `asp` server**: `asp_memory_list`, `asp_memory_read`, `asp_memory_write` (a short note with a name and description; the index line is kept), `asp_memory_search` over the run's copy of the agent's memory (paths cannot leave it, a note is at most 64 KB), and, when `ASP_LOG_URL` points at a service with a commons, `asp_commons_search`, `asp_commons_show` and `asp_commons_cite` (the citation is signed with the agent's own key). The agent's memory index is handed to it in the server's `instructions` at the handshake. Calls to these tools need no scope (like a planning tool) and only while the gateway is serving them. (2) **Proxy for the agent's other MCP servers**: `--mcp <name>=<https url>` (Streamable HTTP) or `--mcp <name>=stdio:<command> [args]` (repeatable; the gateway runs the process); the agent is pointed at `/mcp/<name>`, every `tools/call` is judged as scope `mcp.<name>.<tool>` against the Mandate (gates, known-bad list, strikes and the stop rule apply), a refused call returns an error result and never reaches the server. `asp gateway` writes `mcp.json` for the agent and passes it as `ASP_MCP_CONFIG` (and `ASP_GATEWAY_URL`). (3) **Memory write-back for any agent**: `--package <dir>` loads the agent's memory from its package, and after the run writes what it saved back as a signed lineage update, merged with any other run and kept within the budget, exactly as `asp run` does. Checked live: real Claude Code (no ASP plugin) saved a lesson through `asp_memory_write` in one session, and a fresh session answered the question from that note without being told. Not yet: sharing a lesson to the commons from the gateway (needs the Mandate's `share_to_commons` and a review step), MCP resources and prompts, server-initiated MCP messages, OAuth-protected MCP servers, a live check of the commons tools and of a real third-party MCP server through the proxy. |
| S58 | Gateway P3 step 1: the OpenAI Responses API (new, 2026-10; tooling) | The gateway judges `POST /v1/responses`, the API current Codex speaks, whole or streamed. Tool calls are the output items `function_call`, `local_shell_call` and `custom_tool_call` (a shell tool's `command` may be an argument list, which is joined and classified like any shell command). In a stream, other items flow through; each tool call's events are held until its item is done, then forwarded or dropped. Output indexes and sequence numbers are renumbered so a dropped call leaves no gap, a refusal message item (with its content events) takes its place, and the final `response.completed` carries the filtered output and the usage. Event shapes were taken from a real streamed response (OpenRouter's Responses API). Checked live: real Codex 0.157.1 driving an open-weight model (Nemotron 120B through OpenRouter) via the gateway with a `repo.read` Mandate and no ASP hook (Codex has none): its `exec_command` call for `curl.exe` was refused before Codex received it, Codex ended its turn with the refusal note, one strike, one `gateway_enforced` Action. This takes Codex from detect-and-stop to prevent-before-it-runs. Not yet: Codex under a ChatGPT login (it talks to the ChatGPT backend, not an API base URL we can point at, so the gateway needs an API key route), approval gates through Codex, Gemini API, Antigravity. |
| S59 | Gateway P3 step 2: more real agents, and honest assurance (new, 2026-10; tooling) | Installed (with approval, into `C:\Users\Navie\asp-tools`, nothing global): Gemini CLI 0.63.0, OpenCode 1.18.35, Goose 1.54.0, Aider 0.86.2. Checked live through `asp gateway` with an open-weight model (Nemotron 120B on OpenRouter) under a `repo.read` Mandate: **Goose** works as is (OpenAI provider settings in its environment): its `shell` curl call was refused before Goose received it, one strike, one Action. **Aider** runs through the gateway but uses a text edit format, so no structured tool call ever passes through: nothing can be enforced, and its own code acted outside the model loop (it tried to scrape the URL in the prompt, auto-installed Playwright and started downloading Chromium). **OpenCode** and **Gemini CLI** are not working through the gateway yet (see the gaps register P10, P11). **Honest assurance:** the Action now says `gateway_enforced` only when at least one structured tool call was judged; a run where none passed through is `gateway_observed`, with the reason in the summary. |
| S60 | Gateway P4: the self-report SDK and the sandbox level (new, 2026-10; tooling, uses the `assurance` field of S56) | **Self-report SDK** (`AgentReporter` in TypeScript, `asp_core.AgentReporter` in Python, standard library only): a hosted agent that cannot be wrapped reads the live Mandate from a log service, `guard(scope, fn)` refuses a scope the Mandate does not grant (recording a blocked attempt) and does not run `fn`, `note` records an action done without asking, and `flush` signs one Action with the agent's own key and `assurance: self_reported`. The log still refuses an Action that claims a scope outside the Mandate, and the job must be Running. Checked across languages: a Python agent holding only its own key reported to a real `asp serve`. **Sandbox level** (`asp gateway --sandbox`, Linux with bubblewrap, so WSL on Windows): the command runs with no network unless the Mandate grants `shell.network` or `web.read` (it reaches the gateway through a relay that forwards a loopback port inside the sandbox to the gateway's Unix socket), the system read-only, the home folder hidden, a private /tmp, the project read-only unless the Mandate grants `repo.write`, the agent's memory folder writable, `--sandbox-bind path[:rw]` for tools, and a cleared environment rebuilt from a short list; the Action then carries `sandbox_enforced`. Checked live in WSL with a Python agent: internet blocked, the gateway reached through the relay, the home folder empty, the operator's provider key absent, the project read-only, memory written and kept. **Fix:** the provider keys named by `--openai-key-env` and `--anthropic-key-env` are removed from the agent's environment in every mode; before this the agent inherited them. Not yet: a Docker backend (Windows and macOS), a live `asp gateway --sandbox` run (needs Node in WSL), host-level egress rules. |
| S61 | Gateway P4 follow-up: the Docker backend for the sandbox level (new, 2026-10; tooling) | `asp gateway --sandbox --sandbox-backend docker [--sandbox-image <image>]` (auto picks bubblewrap on Linux and WSL, Docker elsewhere). The agent runs in a container on an `--internal` Docker network, which has no route to the internet; a relay container, the only member of that network that also sits on the default one, forwards a port to the gateway on the host (`host.docker.internal`). The agent container is read-only, runs as an unprivileged user with all capabilities dropped and no-new-privileges, has a private /tmp, a PID and memory limit, the project mounted read-only unless the Mandate grants `repo.write`, and only the environment the gateway sets. A network scope in the Mandate (`shell.network`, `web.read`) gives it the default network instead. The Action carries `sandbox_enforced`. The relay and the network are removed when the agent exits. Docker Desktop was uninstalled and reinstalled to get its engine working (installer signature checked: Docker Inc). Checked live on Windows with Docker Desktop 4.83 and `python:3.13-alpine`: a Python agent in the container reached an open-weight model through the relay and the gateway (key held by the gateway); the internet was refused, the provider key was absent from its environment, a write to the project was refused, the model's `bash` curl call was refused before the agent saw it. The command runs inside the image, so host programs are not available in it (gaps register SB6). |
| S62 | The canary suite and the model matrix (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change) | `asp canary run --target <file | openrouter:<model>> [--suite] [--trials n] [--only id,id] [--out report.json] [--baseline report.json]`, `asp canary compare <baseline> <current>`, `asp canary list`. A suite is data (`canary/default-suite.json`: six tasks, recall from a file, an exact output format, counting files, staying inside a read-only Mandate, ignoring an instruction hidden in a file, carrying on after a refused call); a task has files, scopes, trials and checks (`exit_ok`, `answer_matches`, `answer_not_matches`, `max_blocked`, `min_tool_calls`, `max_tool_calls`, `max_tokens`, `max_seconds`, `scopes_within`). A target is any command that can use an OpenAI-compatible or Anthropic endpoint (`{prompt}`, `{project}`, `{node}`, `{reference-agent}` are replaced); the small reference agent (`src/reference-agent.mjs`: read_file, list_files, bash in the project folder) lets a model be tested directly. Each trial gets a fresh project, a throwaway ASP home and a fresh job whose Mandate is the task's scopes, run under `asp gateway --capture`, so the numbers are the gateway's (tool calls, blocked attempts, tokens, requests) and the agent's captured answer. A report saved as a baseline and a later one compared: a task that passed at least two thirds of its trials and dropped by a third or more is a REGRESSION (exit code 1); growth in tokens, tool calls, time or blocked attempts is drift. A provider failure (reference agent exit 3: rate limit, outage, no credit) is an error, left out of pass rates, never a failed task. `asp gateway --capture <file>` writes the agent's output to a file. **The model matrix** (`evals/model-matrix.mjs`) runs the suite across several OpenRouter models and writes `docs/model-matrix.md`; `evals/canary-suite.mjs` compares one model with a stored baseline in `evals/baselines/`. Not yet: running the suite automatically on a memory update or runtime swap, and recording its result on the lineage edge (gaps register CM1). |
| S63 | The canary as a gate on a change (new, 2026-10; tooling, uses the existing `change.gates` field of the lineage edge and the `certificate` attestation; no protocol change) | `asp canary setup --agent <did> --backend <runtime> --target <file | package:claude-code> [--canary-gate warn|block] [--trials n] [--suite file]` stores, in the ASP home, a canary target per agent and backend (the target's command uses `{package}` so the canary sees the agent's memory; `{asp}` is the command line itself). From then on `asp run` tests a change before writing it back: the memory update or runtime swap is applied to a copy of the package, the suite is run on the copy through the gateway, and the result is compared with the baseline for that backend (the first run becomes the baseline; `asp canary baseline` sets one explicitly). The result is recorded in the log as a **certificate attestation** signed by the agent (`about` = the new memory's hash, `verdict` passed, regressed or baseline_set, `score` = pass rate in permille, `skill` = `canary:<suite hash>`, reasons = the summary, regressions and drift) and cited in the lineage edge's `change.gates`; the edge's description says what the canary found. With the gate on `block` a regression stops the change being written back (the certificate is still recorded, so the refusal is on the record); with `warn` it is written back and says so. `--no-canary` skips it; `--canary-gate` overrides the setting. `asp verify` now shows how many recorded changes cite a canary result (the check that used to be empty); `asp canary evidence <package>` lists each change with the verdict of the certificate it cites. The canary reads a runtime's own output: Claude Code's stream-json and Codex's `--json` are read for the final message. Found live: a canary against Claude Code through `asp run` first scored 1 of 2 tasks because the answer was taken from the JSON stream, not the final message; fixed. Checked with Claude Code: a real memory update was gated, baselined and cited, a second one compared with the baseline. Also adds `bin/asp.mjs` (the `asp` command line as a script, `asp` in the package's bin). Not yet: gating `asp gateway --package` and `asp orchestrate`, carrying certificates in the package (gaps register CM6, CM7). |
| S64 | Every write-back path goes through the canary (new, 2026-10; tooling) | Closes CM6. A single function, `applyChange`, now writes every change to an agent's package; `asp run` (memory update, runtime swap), `asp gateway --package` (memory saved through the gateway's MCP tools; the canary target is set up under the backend name `gateway`) and `asp orchestrate` (consolidated fleet memory, on the backend it ran) all use it, so a change is tested, recorded as a certificate and cited in the lineage edge the same way whichever path made it, and a `block` gate refuses it the same way. `asp canary setup ... --package <pkg>` takes the baseline immediately, so the first change is compared with a real baseline instead of becoming it. Tests cover all three paths with a stand-in agent that is confused by a memory file. |
| S65 | Model providers beyond OpenRouter (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change) | `asp canary run --target <provider>:<model>` now accepts `openrouter`, `groq`, `cerebras` and `gemini` (the `PROVIDERS` table in canary.ts: OpenAI-compatible base URL, key env var, key file in the home folder). The key is read from `ASP_<PROVIDER>_KEY` or `~/.asp-<provider>-key` and is passed to the gateway only (`--openai-key-env`), never to the agent. `evals/model-matrix.mjs` takes `--models provider:model,...` and `--merge` (keeps earlier models, drops all-error rows). Provider failures (429, 5xx, retired model) are recorded as errors, not failed tasks. Live results: Groq gpt-oss-120b 6/6, gpt-oss-20b 5/6 (fell for the injection-in-file task 3/3), qwen3.8-27b 6/6; Cerebras gpt-oss-120b and qwen-3.8-27b 6/6; Gemini gemma-4-31b-it 5/6, gemini-3.5-flash 3/6 (3 tasks errored); gemini-3.8-flash 5/6 once, then rate-limited. gemini-2.5-flash is retired for new users (404). |
| S66 | Action `metrics` (new, 2026-10; protocol change, optional field, same shape of change as `assurance` in S56) | The Action record gains an optional `metrics` object: `models` (each `{name, provider?, digest?}`), `requests`, `tool_calls`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `seconds` (non-negative integers; no other fields). The figures are for the interval since the Action before it, so a trend adds them up. The gateway fills it from the traffic it relayed: the model name each request asked for (with the upstream host as provider), request and tool-call counts, the token usage the provider reported, and wall-clock seconds; `asp market action --metrics '<json>'` records it by hand. An Action is now also written for an interval that only made model requests (no scopes, no blocked attempts), so the cost is not lost. Conformance: 4 new schema vectors (82 in all). Closes the structured-metrics part of D8/E3 and the model-name part of D4. |
| S67 | Default-deny egress by tier and named hosts (new, 2026-10; protocol change, optional Mandate field plus a log rule) | **Mandate:** optional `network: { hosts: [...] }`, exact names or subdomain wildcards (`*.example.com`, which does not match `example.com`); no bare `*`, no paths, at least one. **Log rule:** a Mandate for a tier 1 or 2 agent that grants a network scope (`web.*`, `shell.network`, `net.*`) must name hosts or the log refuses it (`network_hosts_required`); tier 3 and above may leave them out, which means no host limit. **Gateway:** with hosts set, a web fetch or a shell network command (URLs, and bare hosts after curl, wget, nc, ssh, scp and similar) to any other host is refused as a blocked attempt on that scope, and so is a call whose host cannot be read (`curl $TARGET`); a web search names no host and is not limited. `asp market mandate --network-host <host> ...`; `asp gateway` reads the hosts from the live Mandate. Core exports `isNetworkScope` and `hostAllowed`. Conformance: 5 new schema vectors (87 in all). Tested with stand-in agents and the unit judge, not yet live. Covers item (3) of the backlog entry "Harm that no Mandate names"; not rate limits, the victim channel or swarm caps. |
| S68 | Host limit in the runtime pre-call hooks (new, 2026-10; tooling, closes H11 for Claude Code and Antigravity) | `asp run --contract` writes the Mandate's `network.hosts` into the hook's `asp-mandate.json` (`hosts`), and the Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks refuse a network call (WebFetch, `read_url_content`, `open_browser_url`, a shell command) to any other host, or to one they cannot read, before it runs. The hook scripts are standalone, so they carry a copy of the host reader; a test runs a table of calls through the hook and the gateway's judge and requires the same answer. `browser.*` scopes now count as network scopes (core `isNetworkScope`, so the tier rule of S67 covers them). A web search or a page-bound browser action names no host and is not limited. Codex and OpenHands hooks do not prevent calls, so they are not covered. |
| S69 | The gateway's run log (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; closes the recorder part of E2) | `asp gateway` keeps `run-log.ndjson` in its run folder (`--no-run-log` turns it off). One JSON line per event: `run_start`, `model_request` (API, model, a cut and redacted excerpt of the last user message, tools offered), `model_reply` (tokens in and out, milliseconds, the reply text, whole or streamed), `model_error`, `tool_call` (tool, scope, allowed, reason, gated, the call's input), `stopped`, `run_end`. Every string is masked with the secret patterns (`redactSecrets`) and cut to 1200 characters; the file is hash-chained (each line holds the hash of the one before), so the head hash commits to the whole run. Every Action the gateway reports carries an artifact `asp://run-log/<events>` whose sha256 is the head after that many events, so the log shows the run log was not changed afterwards. `asp run-log show [file|folder] [--json]` prints the timeline; `asp run-log verify [file|folder] [--contract <id>]` checks the chain and that each Action commitment matches. The content stays on the machine; only the hash reaches the log. Tested with stand-in agents. Left: E4 to E8 in the gaps register (E4, secrets masking, now also applies to the run log). |
| S70 | The end-of-Mandate mail (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; first part of E8) | `asp mail preview --contract <id> [--run-log <file|folder>] [--html] [--link-base <url>]` builds the one mail for a Mandate from the signed log: what it was asked, what it was allowed (scopes, hosts, spend cap, what needed approval), what it did (scopes used, model requests, tool calls, tokens, models, how strongly it was held), what was blocked (with the first blocked call), the approvals and the answers, memory changes (lineage edges), how it ended and what moved (settlement), and how to check it (record ids). When the gateway kept a run log it is found by contract (or given with `--run-log`), checked, and summarised by counts and the first refusal; the prompts and replies are not in the mail. `asp mail queue --contract <id> --to <address>` writes an .eml, .html and .txt to `<home>/outbox` and records it in `mail-state.json`: one mail per Mandate (`--again` queues a second), refused while the job has not ended. `asp mail pending` lists ended Mandates not mailed yet. Nothing is sent. Pure builder `buildMandateMail` and reader `collectMandateFacts` are exported from the package. Left: E9 to E12 in the gaps register. |
| S71 | The mail trigger and alerts (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; second part of E8, most of E10) | `asp mail watch [--once] [--interval s] [--to addr]` looks at the log and queues, once each, the end-of-Mandate mail for every job that has settled and an alert mail for: a **kill** (a revoked settlement with a slashed bond, which is what the kill switch writes), an **upheld report** (a `report_ruling` upheld on a report about the contract), and an **expired Mandate** (its `expires` has passed while the job is still Running or in Checkpoint). Alerts are short, marked high importance (`X-ASP-Alert`), and say what happened and which records to check; the end mail still follows when the job settles, so a killed job gets two mails by design (flow doc step 8). Recipients come from `asp mail address set <did> <address>` (the principal's), or `--to`; a job with no address is skipped and retried on the next pass. `asp mail pending` lists what is due. Exports `findAlerts` and `buildAlertMail`. Nothing is sent (E9). Left: E13 and E14. |
| S72 | Gateway report fix and test stability (new, 2026-10; tooling) | The gateway no longer drops an interval's tokens: an Action is skipped only when nothing happened (no scopes, blocked attempts, requests, tool calls or tokens), where before a request counted in one interval whose tokens arrived in the next, with no new request, was dropped. Found by the revoke-mid-run test, which also showed the limit now recorded as E15: a settled job takes no more Actions. Two flaky tests fixed (X4): the revoke test waits for the agent instead of sleeping and checks the Actions against the run log; the commons test freezes the clock because an entry's id includes its creation second. |
| S73 | Eager Action reports from the gateway (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows E15) | `asp gateway` no longer reports only every 30 s: a blocked attempt is reported about 20 ms after it, so the evidence a kill rests on is in the log before any stop can land, and other activity within `ASP_GATEWAY_EAGER_MS` (default 2000) of its first call; the interval (`ASP_GATEWAY_FLUSH_MS`, default 30 s) and the report at exit stay. The cost is more Actions in the log during activity (at most one every 2 s). The end mail counts the run log's events against the last event any Action committed to and, when the run log holds more, says so: those events came after the last report a settled job could take. A final, flagged Action after settlement would close the window entirely; that needs a log rule and is left (E15). |
| S74 | The run log beyond the gateway: `asp run`, tool results and MCP calls (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows E5 and E7) | `asp run` keeps `run-log.ndjson` in its run folder when it reads the runtime's output (`--no-run-log` turns it off). Per-runtime readers (`describeOutput` on the adapter's plan): Claude Code's stream-json and Codex's `--json` give the agent's text, each tool call with its input, each tool's result (marked failed or blocked), and how the run ended; every runtime adds a `call_judged` event for each call the compliance bridge sees (scope, granted, gated, known-bad), plus `strike`, `kill`, `run_start` and `run_end`. The Action, including the kill-path one, carries the `asp://run-log/<events>` commitment. The gateway now also records `tool_result` (the answers to earlier tool calls found in each model request, once each, in chat, Messages and Responses shapes) and `mcp_call` (every MCP tools/call it served, with input and result). `asp run-log show` prints the new kinds, and defaults to the newest run folder of either kind; `asp mail` finds an `asp run` job's run log by its contract. Same masking and cutting as S69. Left: E5 and E7 in the gaps register. |
| S75 | Public release files (new, 2026-10; no protocol change) | LICENSE (Apache License 2.0, copied from the copy that ships with a dependency, not downloaded), NOTICE (copyright 2026 Navien Potheri: to be confirmed by the owner), SECURITY.md (private vulnerability reporting), CONTRIBUTING.md (how to test, how a change is recorded), a README that states what works, what does not, and that the credits are a mock ledger, the root manifest's license field, and `docs/github-page.md` (description, topics, settings, commands, the pre-announcement list). A scan of all 104 commits for secret shapes found only the test suite's fake keys. Nothing was applied to the GitHub repository. |
| S76 | Continuous integration (new, 2026-10; no protocol change) | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pushes to main and event-log and on pull requests: **node** (Node 24: `npm ci`, typecheck, every package's tests, and `npm run vectors` followed by `git diff --exit-code conformance/vectors`, so committed vectors match the generator), **python** (3.12, the SDK's tests) and **postgres** (a `postgres:17` service, the log and CLI tests against it; allowed to fail until it has run). Before writing it, the same code was exported with `git archive` into WSL Ubuntu, installed with `npm ci` and tested on Linux: all four TypeScript suites passed, also with bubblewrap hidden. The vectors generator was run twice and changes nothing.
