It ran on GitHub on 2026-10-10 (run 37977449485 for the push and 37977449537 for pull request 1): node, python and postgres all passed, so the Python SDK on Linux and the log against real Postgres 17 are now checked. |
| S77 | TLS and rate limits for the log service (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; closes O1, narrows O2) | **TLS:** `asp serve --tls-cert <pem> --tls-key <pem>` serves HTTPS and sends `Strict-Transport-Security`; the two go together; bound to anything but loopback without TLS the service refuses to start unless `--allow-plain-http` (TLS terminated by a proxy in front). A client of a private certificate sets `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`. **Limits** (on by default in `asp serve`, off in the library unless `limits` is passed): `--rate-limit` (600 requests a minute per tenant, burst a quarter of that), `--write-limit` (120 writes a minute: append and import), `--address-limit` (1200 a minute per address, signed in or not), `--max-in-flight` (16 per tenant), `--max-failed-auth` (10 failed sign-ins in 5 minutes lock the address out for 5 minutes, even for a right token); 0 turns one off; a tenant's `rateLimitPerMinute` replaces the default for it; `--trust-proxy` reads the client address from X-Forwarded-For. A refused request gets HTTP 429, `Retry-After`, and `{error: {code: RATE_LIMITED, message, retryAfterSec}}`; refusal happens before the call runs, so repeating it is safe, and the log client (`RemoteLog`) waits and repeats up to 3 times. In memory and per process; idle entries are swept. Left: O2, O15, O16. |
| S78 | Every service client waits out a rate limit (new, 2026-10; tooling; closes O16) | `fetchRetry(input, init, retries = 3)` in asp-package: on HTTP 429 it waits the `Retry-After` the service sent (at least 1 s, at most 10 s), then sends the same request again, and after the last try returns the 429 for the caller to report. Safe because the service refuses before it runs anything. Used by `RemoteLog` (replacing its own loop), `PackagesClient`, `fetchKnownBad` and `postKnownBad`, and the commons calls in the CLI. Tests: the helper against a stand-in that sends 429 once (same body twice) and always (gives up), and the three clients against a real service with a limit already used up. |
| S79 | Tenant quotas and operator controls for the log service (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows O2) | **Quota:** the service counts the records and bytes each tenant has had accepted (append: 1 record; import: the number imported; bytes: the size of the call's arguments) and refuses a write that would pass the tenant's quota with HTTP 403 `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` before running it. Defaults on `asp serve`: `--record-quota` 100000 and `--byte-quota-mb` 256 (0 for none); `asp serve token --record-quota n --byte-quota-mb n --rate-limit n` gives one tenant its own; admins have none; a refused write is not counted. Counts are kept in `<tokens file>.usage.json` (written within a second, and when the service closes). **Suspend:** `asp serve suspend --tokens f --tenant name [--reason text]` / `resume` set or clear the tenant's `suspended` field; the service refuses all its requests with 403 `SUSPENDED` and the reason. **Block:** `asp serve block --tokens f --address ip [--minutes n] [--reason text]` / `unblock` edit `<tokens file>.blocked.json`; a blocked address gets 403 `BLOCKED` before anything else. The service re-reads the tokens file and the block list when they change, so none of this needs a restart, and a token created with `asp serve token` works at once. `asp serve usage --tokens f` prints each tenant's records and bytes against its quota, who is suspended, and the blocked addresses. The log client no longer says "set ASP_LOG_TOKEN" for a 403. Left: O2, O17, O18. |
| S80 | The late Action (new, 2026-10; protocol change: an optional Action field and a log rule; closes E15) | The Action record gains an optional `late: { activity_ended }`. **Rule:** an Action without `late` is accepted only while the job is Running or in Checkpoint, as before. One with `late` is accepted only if (1) the job is Settled, (2) its issuer is the performer, (3) `activity_ended` is no later than 30 seconds after the settlement's `issued_at`, and (4) the Action's own `issued_at` is no later than ten minutes after the settlement's. Everything else about an Action still holds: the scopes it names must be in the Mandate (`scope_violation` otherwise) and its blocked attempts count as strikes. A `late` Action on a job that is still running is refused (`late_action_while_running`); the others fail with `late_action_after_end` or `late_action_too_late`. The job's state, the ledger and the chain do not change (an Action is standalone). **Tooling:** `asp market action --late <time>`; the gateway tracks when it last saw activity, and when its report finds the job ended ('is not currently Running') it repeats the report with `--late <that time>`, so a kill, revoke or settlement no longer loses the last interval (the revoke-mid-run test now requires the Actions to add up exactly to the run log). Conformance: 5 new schema vectors (92 in all). Left: E16. |
| S81 | Late Action follow-ups (new, 2026-10; a log rule and a migration; closes E16) | **Limit:** a job may take at most 5 late Actions; the sixth is refused (`late_action_limit`). The count is kept on the Mandate row (`late_actions`, Postgres migration 013) and rebuilt by replay, so `verify()` agrees. **Mail:** the end-of-Mandate mail says how many of the reports were made after the job ended. **Reporters:** the TypeScript and Python self-report SDKs, and `asp run` at the end of a run, repeat a report the log refused with 'is not currently Running' as a late Action, with `activity_ended` the time of the agent's last activity (the run's end for `asp run`); the log accepts it only inside the S80 window or refuses it. The reporters keep what they recorded if the late report is refused too. Left by design: the 30 s slack, and Courts and verifiers treat a late Action like any other. |
| S82 | CI on Windows, Node 22.18 and Python 3.11 (new, 2026-10; no protocol change; narrows O14) | The workflow runs the TypeScript packages on ubuntu-latest and windows-latest with Node 24 and 22.18, and the Python SDK on Ubuntu with 3.12 and 3.11 and on Windows with 3.12, plus Postgres 17; each combination is its own named check. First run on `ca0e583`: Ubuntu and Python all green; Windows failed one test on both Node versions (asp-cli, OpenHands launch command): the runner's short temp path (`RUNNER~1`) made the printed command quoted, and the test's pattern did not allow quotes, a test fix and no code change. Run 38012008700 on `0388634`: all eight green, so none is marked experimental any more and Postgres no longer has `continue-on-error`. Node 22.18 is now verified as the minimum. |
| S83 | Atomic approval decisions (new, 2026-10; tooling) | `asp run` writes the principal's answer to a Checkpoint (`<id>.decision.json` in the run's approvals folder) to a temporary name and renames it, so it appears whole or not at all. The Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks and the gateway treat a decision file that does not parse as not yet answered and read it again on the next poll (before, they crashed or failed closed on it); an answer that stays unreadable is refused when the wait runs out, with the usual 'no answer' reason. Found by the Postgres CI job, which became a required check in S82, in a gate test that failed once with the hook's 'Unexpected end of JSON input'. Tests: the hook and the gateway given a half-written file that is completed 250 to 300 ms later approve; an unreadable one is refused at the end of the wait. |
| S84 | The dashboard (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows U2, U7, U8, closes U3 and U6) | `asp dashboard [--port n]` serves one page and a JSON API on 127.0.0.1 only, reading the log in the ASP home (or the service named by `ASP_LOG_URL`) afresh on each request. It refuses a request whose Host is not this machine, and every `/api` call needs the random token printed at start (kept in the tab's session storage, taken out of the address). **Screens:** Home (agents with tier, credits, strikes and blocked calls; jobs with state; counts), a job page (what it was allowed and did, the agent's reports, approvals, the run log with refused calls marked, the chain, the money it holds and every flow, a "with real money" panel, record ids and the command to check them), Approvals, Alerts (S71's kill, upheld report and expiry, and the latest blocked attempts), Money. **API:** `/api/home`, `/api/job?id=`, `/api/inbox`, `/api/alerts`, `/api/money`, `/api/verify`, and `POST /api/resolve {contract, verdict: approved|refused, reason}`, which signs a `checkpoint_resolution` with the principal's own key from the ASP home (refusal is the `corrected` verdict with the reason as the correction) and fails if that key is not on this machine. **Money check:** minted = balances of every account the log knows + escrow and bond locked in jobs that have not ended; shown as Conserved or NOT conserved with the difference. The data comes from `dashboard*` functions in asp-package built on the same facts as the mail; the mail's run-page link now points at `/#/job/<id>`. `evals/dashboard-demo.mjs` builds a home to look at. Left: U2, U7, U8, U11, U12. |
| S85 | The agent page (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows U2) | `GET /api/agent?did=` and the screen `#/agent/<did>` in `asp dashboard`; every name in the dashboard now links to it. **For an agent:** who stands behind it (sponsor, earnings split, declared tier and shape, what it was copied from and its copies), its tier, strikes this month and slash count, probation, credits, what it has earned and forfeited, the scopes it used and was blocked from, its models and tokens, its **lineage** (every update, with its layer, description, probation and the **canary certificates** cited as gates, shown as a verdict and a score), its jobs as performer, **reports** filed against it with their status, its keys (in use or revoked) and passport versions. **For a person:** the agents they sponsor, the jobs they hired for, their keys. Built in asp-package (`dashboardAgent`) from the same records as the other screens; the `report` and `passport`, `keys`, `fleet`, `probation` reads were added to the dashboard's view of the log. Left: U13, P15. |
| S86 | The signed fork edge (new, 2026-10; protocol change: a log rule on the passport's existing `lineage` field; closes P15) | A passport whose `lineage` has `{edge: "fork", parent: <agent DID>}` says its subject is an independent copy of that agent. **Log rules, on the first passport:** the subject is an agent (`fork_not_agent`); not a copy of itself (`fork_self`); one fork parent (`fork_one_parent`); the parent is an agent with a passport in the log (`fork_parent_unknown`); the passport names the parent's sponsor and is issued by that sponsor, not by the agent itself (`fork_needs_sponsor`); the parent is not at tier 0 (`fork_from_excluded`, the rule `asp identity copy` already applied, now enforced by the log); the copy declares no higher tier than the parent has now (`fork_tier_above_parent`). **Later versions** of the passport must carry the same fork edges (`fork_immutable`). Nothing is inherited: a copy has its own account, record and strikes. `asp identity copy` now writes the edge (and keeps its purpose text); a package's history follows fork parents so the original's passport is in it and the package verifies; the dashboard's agent page shows what a copy was copied from and an original's copies from the edge. Conformance: 2 passport vectors (94 schema vectors in all). Left: P16, P17. |
| S87 | The sponsor's consent (new, 2026-10; protocol change: a log rule on the passport; closes P16) | A passport that names a `sponsor`, when it is the passport's first version or changes the sponsor, must be signed by that sponsor: the sponsor is the issuer, or one of the countersignatures (`cosigs`) is made with a key of the sponsor's DID. Otherwise the log refuses it with `sponsor_consent_missing`. When the passport already has a sponsor and moves to another, the old sponsor must also sign (as issuer or countersigner): `sponsor_change_needs_old_sponsor`. The older rules stand: the issuer is the DID itself or its current sponsor (`WRONG_ISSUER`), so a new sponsor cannot issue the move on its own, and an agent may still update its own passport (a purpose, a key) while the sponsor stays the same. The rule is on every passport kind that names a sponsor. Why: the sponsor is liable for the agent and its reputation attaches to it, so no agent can choose who answers for it, and a sponsor can see exactly which agents it signed for. `asp identity new` and `asp identity copy` already issued agent passports with the sponsor's key. Left: P18. |
| S88 | Packages, memory and commons on the agent page (new, 2026-10; tooling; the service's package listing gains fields; closes U13) | **Package facts:** `packageMeta` reads a package's signed manifest and counts its memory (topic files, bytes, index lines), skills and source runtime and model; `scanPackages(folder)` finds `*.aspkg` folders and `*.aspkg.tgz` or `*.tar.gz` archives. **Service:** the package service writes a sidecar `<name>.meta.json` (keyed by the archive's ETag) when a package is uploaded, builds one on first listing for a package stored earlier, deletes it with the package, and `GET /packages` now returns `agent` and `meta` for each package. **Dashboard:** `GET /api/agent` adds `packages`, `commons` and `notes`; `asp dashboard --packages <folder>` reads a folder, and with `ASP_LOG_URL` it reads the service's packages (this token's tenant) and the commons entries the agent authored (title, tags, status, endorsements, disputes, citations). The page shows each package with its memory as bars against the default budget (warning over 80%, red over 100%) and says in `notes` what it could not read, rather than showing an empty list. Left: U14. |
| S89 | Per-host rate limits (new, 2026-10; protocol change: an optional Mandate field and an optional metrics field; narrows H1) | The Mandate's `network` object gains `rate: { per_host_per_minute?, total_per_minute? }` (whole numbers, 1 or more, at least one); `network` now needs `hosts` or `rate` (or both). The Action's `metrics` gains `rate_limited`. **Enforcement:** a network call (a scope that `isNetworkScope` names) is counted per host and in total over a sliding minute; one that would pass a limit is refused, **without a strike**, and counted in `metrics.rate_limited`; a refused call is not counted against the window. The host is read the way the host limit reads it (URLs, and bare hosts after curl, wget, nc, ssh and the like); a call whose host cannot be read counts only toward the total. The gateway keeps the window in memory (`RateLimiter`); the Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks, which are a new process per call, keep it in `rate-calls.ndjson` in the run folder and write each refusal to `rate-limited.ndjson` (not `blocked-calls.ndjson`, and the message says 'ASP rate limit', not 'ASP Mandate', so `asp run` does not read it as a block); `asp run` counts that file into the Action's metrics. `asp market mandate --network-rate-per-host n --network-rate-total n`. The end mail and the dashboard's job page show the limit and how many calls it refused. Conformance: 7 new schema vectors (101 in all). Left: H1, H15, H16. |
| S90 | The rate limit counts requests, not calls (2026-10; no schema change; narrows H17) | A network call counts as the requests it makes. For a shell command the gateway (`gateway/requests.ts`) and the Claude Code and Antigravity hooks (a copy, kept in step by a test on a table of 16 commands) read: each URL or host named (a curl range `page[1-50]` or brace list `page{1..8}` as many), a loop around a network command (`for x in a b c`, `seq`, `for ((...))`, PowerShell `1..20 | ForEach-Object`) as many times as it runs, and an unbounded loop (`while`, `until`, `xargs`, `parallel`, `wget -r`, a `ping` with no count) or a scanner or flood tool (nmap, hydra, ffuf, ab, wrk and the like) as 1000, which any limit refuses; `ping -c 4` counts 4. A call over a limit is refused whole, with the number in the reason ('this call makes about 8 requests to HOST'); a refused call is not counted. The hook ledger line is now `{t, perHost, total}`. A script that makes its own requests (python scrape.py) still counts as one. Live: evals/rate-limit.mjs part two, real Claude Code, all eight curls in one Bash call: refused whole, the site saw none. Left: H17 (scripts), H15, H16. |
| S91 | Real runtimes as canary targets (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows CM3) | `asp canary run --target runtime:claude-code[:model]` runs the Claude Code CLI on the user's own login (the gateway passes it through to api.anthropic.com; default claude-haiku-5-5) and `runtime:codex[:model]` runs the Codex CLI through OpenRouter's Responses API (default openai/gpt-oss-120b; `src/codex-runtime.mjs` points it at the gateway). A target may carry `tokenScale` (multiplies the task's max_tokens, because a runtime sends its own long prompt: 4 for Claude Code, 10 for Codex) and `extraScopes` (granted to the Mandate and allowed by the scope check: Codex has only a shell, so reading a file is shell.exec). `evals/model-matrix.mjs` accepts `runtime:` names. Left: CM3. |
| S92 | Safe defaults for network access (new, 2026-10; protocol change: a log rule; tooling: egress proxy, default rate; narrows H13, H15, H16) | **Log rule:** a Mandate for a tier 1 or 2 agent that grants a network scope must carry `network.rate.per_host_per_minute` as well as hosts, or the log refuses it (`network_rate_required`); tier 3 and above may leave it out. **Default:** `asp market mandate` gives a Mandate with a network scope and no rate named the default rate (`DEFAULT_NETWORK_RATE`: 60 a minute to any one host, 300 in all) unless `--network-rate-unlimited` (which the log refuses for tier 1 and 2). **Egress proxy (H13):** with `--sandbox`, a Mandate that names hosts or a rate leaves the sandbox with no network of its own (before, it got the host's whole network); the gateway answers HTTP proxy requests, CONNECT tunnels and absolute-URI HTTP, on the same port or socket the relay already reaches, and the agent gets `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY` and `ALL_PROXY` pointing at it (with `NO_PROXY` for the gateway). A connection to a host the Mandate does not list is a blocked attempt (a strike), one over the rate is refused without a strike and counted in `metrics.rate_limited`, a name that resolves to a loopback, private, link-local or metadata address is refused unless the Mandate lists that address (SSRF), and each decision is a `egress` event in the run log. A tool that ignores the proxy settings has no route and fails closed. **Hook ledger (H16):** the Claude Code and Antigravity hooks lock the rate ledger (an exclusive directory next to it, taken over after five seconds) while a call is counted, so a burst of parallel calls is counted one after the other; a test starts 14 processes at once against a limit of 5 and exactly 5 get through. Left: H13 (Docker backend untested live, one request counted per HTTPS tunnel, only HTTP and HTTPS), H15 (a budget shared across a fleet's copies), H18. |
| S93 | Self-serve sign-up for the log service (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows O2) | `asp serve ... --signup --signup-terms-url <url> [--signup-terms-version v] [--signup-difficulty bits] [--signup-per-address n] [--signup-daily-cap n]` opens three unauthenticated routes: `GET /signup` (open or closed, the terms' version and address, the difficulty), `GET /signup/challenge` (a server-signed challenge bound to the asking address, valid ten minutes, usable once) and `POST /signup` `{name, accept_terms: true, terms_version, challenge, nonce, contact?}`. The nonce must make SHA-256 of `<salt>:<nonce>` start with the difficulty's zero bits (default 20, about a second of CPU: a cost for a script that wants thousands of tenants, no third-party CAPTCHA). A name is 3 to 32 characters (lower-case letter first, then letters, digits, hyphens), not reserved, not taken. Limits: 3 sign-ups a day per address and 200 a day in all by default, counted from the tenants file itself (so a restart does not reset them); the address is stored only as a hash. A new tenant is a `tenant` (never admin) with small quotas (2000 records, 16 MB, 120 requests a minute) and records the terms version, the time and an unverified contact line; the token is returned once and only its hash is stored. The operator closes or reopens sign-up and changes the day's cap on a running service: `asp serve signups|signup-open|signup-close [--reason]|signup-cap --signup-daily-cap n --tokens <file>`. Client: `asp signup --service <url> --name <name> --accept-terms [--contact <line>]`. Left: O2 (OAuth sign-in and a verified identity, U1; limits shared across processes; raising a tenant's quotas by something other than the operator's hand). |
| S94 | Sign-up with Google (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; narrows U1, O19) | `asp serve ... --google-client-id <id> --google-redirect-uri <uri> [--signup-require-google] [--public-url <url>]` with the client secret in the environment variable `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` (never a flag). The service answers `GET /join` (form: tenant name, the adult-or-guardian declaration, the terms), `POST /join` (redirects to Google with a signed ten-minute `state` and a cookie nonce) and `GET /auth/callback` (checks the state and the cookie, exchanges the code with the secret, reads the Google account id and email from Google's userinfo endpoint, refuses an unverified email, and shows the new token once on a no-store page). A tenant created this way records `signup.google {sub, email}` and `signup.declaration: adult_or_guardian`; one Google account may hold 3 tenants (a parent for several children); the address and day limits stay. With `--signup-require-google` the proof-of-work route answers `GOOGLE_REQUIRED` and points to /join. The page sets a strict Content-Security-Policy, no scripts, noindex. Privacy notice updated: Google account id and verified email are kept. Left: U1 (a hosted dashboard behind this sign-in, consent for the parent's identity beyond a declaration), O19 (a verified-contact tier and larger quotas). |
| S95 | Account export and deletion for the hosted service (new, 2026-10; tooling and a service route set, no protocol change; narrows U9, launch blocker 1) | The service learns which agent DIDs each tenant has written as (the signer, and the subject, of each record it appends or imports; `<tokens>.dids.json`). `GET /account` shows what it holds; `GET /account/export` returns the tenant (without its token's hash), usage, the DIDs, the records signed by or about them, the commons entries the DIDs wrote and the reviews and citations they made, and the package list (each archive downloads from `/packages/<name>`); `POST /account/close {confirm: <name>}` deletes the tenant's packages, its DIDs' commons entries (with the reviews and citations others made of them) and the reviews and citations they made elsewhere, forgets the DIDs and usage, and turns the tenant entry into a tombstone: no token, no email or contact line, only the name (not reused), the Google account id, the address hash, the terms version and the time, until `retainUntil` (12 months). An admin may close another open non-admin tenant by name (an erasure request). Closed tenants cannot sign in, and no longer count against a Google account's three tenants. CLI: `asp account show|export [--out dir]|close --confirm <name> [--skip-export] [--tenant <name>]` (close exports first unless told not to; export checks each package against its hash); `asp serve purge --tokens <file>` removes tombstones past retention (a nightly cron in the setup script, which also sets journald to 90 days and keeps backups 35 days, as the privacy notice says). The records in the log are permanent; the export and the close both say so. Left: U9 (a page in the browser for closing, retention for local run logs is the user's own, Postgres backups age out only after 35 days). |
| S96 | Starter credits for a newcomer's first job (new, 2026-10; service tooling, no protocol change; narrows M6, launch blocker 3) | Only an operator could mint credits, so a new tenant could post no escrow or bond. A tenant that signed up with a verified Google account may claim a starter grant once: `asp account starter --did <principal>[,<agent>]` (`POST /account/starter`) mints 500 credits to each of up to two identities the tenant has written as. Refused: a tenant with no Google account (operator-made tenants ask the operator), a second claim, a second tenant of the same Google account (the claim stays in a closed account's tombstone, so closing and signing up again does not earn another), an identity the tenant has not written as, more than two identities, and any claim once the pool is spent (`asp serve --starter-pool n`, default 200000; kept in `<tokens>.starter.json`). `asp account show` says whether starter credits are available, claimed or not available and why. A first job within a newcomer's limits (tier 1: Mandate spend up to 100) is covered: price 100, escrow 100, bond 100, accepted and paid, tested end to end through the service. Credits are accounting entries, not money. Left: M6 (a newcomer has jobs to take only from people it knows; discovery of jobs is the society layer), M9. |
| S97 | The operator's court for the beta (new, 2026-10; service policy and tooling, no protocol change; narrows M2, launch blocker 4) | The log service takes a write guard (`createLogServer({guard})`) and `asp serve --jurors operator-only|verified [--juror-min-stake n]` (default operator-only). In operator-only mode only an admin may register a juror (a tenant's registration, or a juror record inside an import, is refused with code POLICY); in verified mode a tenant with a verified Google account may register one identity it wrote as, with at least the minimum stake, one seat for each Google account. In every mode a ruling is refused while no panel can be drawn for its dispute, so the protocol's mocked fallback (any neutral identity may rule when no juror is registered) is never used on the service; the conformance vectors are unchanged because this is policy at the service. Operator commands: `asp court seat [--count 3] [--stake n] [--base did:web:<host>:court]` (creates, funds and registers juror identities, keys kept on the operator's machine), `status` (who is seated, keys held, a warning under 3), `docket` (open disputes and their drawn panels), `rule` (signs with the held keys of the drawn panel, enough for the quorum). The terms say the beta's panel is run by the operator and its rulings move only credits and reputation. Tested end to end through a real service: a tenant refused as juror, a ruling refused before any court, three jurors seated, the dispute found on the docket, ruled by the operator's keys and settled. Left: M2 (community jurors at scale, juror Sybil defence beyond one seat for each Google account), M10. |
| S98 | Mail sent by the service (new, 2026-10; service tooling, no protocol change; narrows E9, E10, M10, launch blocker 6) | `asp serve --mail-from <address> [--mail-operator <address>] [--mail-interval s] [--mail-dry-run]` starts a mailer that reads the log each pass and sends, once each, the end-of-Mandate mail and the alerts (a kill, an upheld report, an expired Mandate) to the principal, and a note to the operator when a dispute opens. The recipient is the verified Google email of the open tenant that wrote as the principal's identity (none: skipped, reason kept). Provider: Resend over HTTPS (`RESEND_API_KEY` in the environment, never a flag); without a key the mail is written to `<tokens>.outbox` and the start line says DRY RUN. Every job mail carries an unsubscribe link and the `List-Unsubscribe` and one-click headers; `GET /mail/unsubscribe` shows a page (a prefetch changes nothing) and `POST` suppresses the address; `POST /mail/webhook/resend` checks the provider's Svix signature (`RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) and suppresses an address on a permanent bounce or a complaint. State (sent, skipped, suppressed, attempts) is kept in `<tokens>.mail.json`, so a restart sends nothing twice; a busy provider is retried up to five times, at most 10 mails an hour go to one address and 50 in a pass. `createLogServer` gains `publicRoutes` for the routes that carry their own proof. Operator notes carry no unsubscribe. Tested through a real service against a stand-in for Resend: the end mail once to the right address with the headers, the unsubscribe link, a second job not mailed, the dispute note to the operator once, a forged webhook refused, a bounce suppressing and a soft bounce not. Left: E9 (live with a real key and a verified domain, DKIM and spam placement unchecked), E11 (links to a run page need blocker 5), E13 (only the principal and the operator are told), M10 (no deadline: a dispute is announced but nothing rules after a time). |
| S99 | Key recovery (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change: it uses the passport's existing support for several keys and for a sponsor issuing an agent's passport; narrows U5, launch blocker 2) | `asp identity new --kind human` now also makes a second key, `<did>#recovery-1`, lists it in the passport and prints it once as a recovery code (`ASP-` and 54 Crockford base32 characters in groups of six, the last two a check) that is never stored on the machine; `--no-recovery` and did:key identities (the DID is the key) have none, and say so. `asp identity recovery-check <did>` proves a written-down code matches (nothing signed); `asp identity recover <did>` replaces a lost key: the recovery key (from `ASP_RECOVERY_CODE` or `--recovery-code-file`, never a flag) signs a passport update that drops every other key and adds a new `#key-N` made on this machine, and the log revokes the dropped keys and the person's live node keys; `--by-sponsor` has an agent's sponsor issue the update. `asp identity backup <did> --out <file>` writes the keys held for an identity encrypted with a passphrase from `ASP_BACKUP_PASSPHRASE` (scrypt, AES-256-GCM, bound to the DID, at least 10 characters, nothing overwritten) and `asp identity restore <file>` puts them back. `asp identity show` reports the recovery state and the keys held here; `asp account show` reports it for each identity a tenant has written as. A did:web identity registered elsewhere must publish the recovery key in its DID document as well. The terms say the service holds no keys. Left: U5 (a browser flow with a reminder to test recovery, social recovery or a server-held encrypted copy, a recovery code for agents, a passkey wrapper, keys are still plaintext files, MOCKS #10). |
| S100 | Identities in the tenant's own namespace (new, 2026-10; service policy and tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12, step 1) | With `--did-domain <host>` (or a `--public-url`, whose host is used) the service gives each tenant a namespace on its domain: a person `did:web:<domain>:u:<tenant>`, further people `...:u:<tenant>:<name>`, agents `...:a:<tenant>:<name>` (a port is encoded `%3A`). A write guard refuses a non-admin tenant's passport (appended or inside an import) for any did:web outside its namespace (code POLICY, with the namespace in the message), so nobody can create an identity that looks like another account's or an unrelated domain's; did:key identities are self-certifying and always allowed, and an admin is not bound (the court's identities, S97). The service publishes each identity's DID document at the address did:web gives it (`GET /u/<tenant>/did.json`, `/u/<tenant>/<name>/did.json`, `/a/<tenant>/<name>/did.json`, `application/did+json`, no sign-in), built from the keys in its current passport (recovery key included) as Ed25519 JWKs, so `fetchDidWebKeys` and `asp identity register` on another service verify them; an unknown identity is 404. `GET /account` and `asp account show` say where a tenant's identities live; `asp identity new --kind human --me`, `--kind human --name <n>` and `--kind agent --name <n>` build the DID from the tenant's namespace on the service named by `ASP_LOG_URL`. The tenant name is public (it is in the DID); the privacy notice says so. Left: a did:web identity on a domain the person owns is refused during the beta (a check that the domain's own document publishes the keys would allow it); identities made before this keep their names; the runtime process itself is tied to its agent only by `asp run` and the gateway, not by the DID. |
| S101 | `asp join`: one command from nothing to a first agent (new, 2026-10; service route and tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12, step 2) | `asp join [--service <url>] [--agent-name <name>] [--no-open] [--yes] [--again]`: the command makes a secret (the verifier), opens `<service>/join?c=<sha256 of it>` in the browser (the address is printed too), and polls `POST /join/pickup {verifier}`. The sign-up page carries the challenge through the form and the signed Google state; after Google, the service creates the tenant as before but, because a challenge is present, holds the token in memory for ten minutes (`JoinPickup`), shows only 'go back to your terminal', and hands the token once to whoever holds the verifier: the browser address (visible on screen, in history) cannot be used to collect it. The command saves `{service, token, tenant}` in `<ASP_HOME>/config.json` (private to the user, plaintext like the key files, MOCKS #10); every later command uses it when `ASP_LOG_URL` is not set (the environment wins), so nothing is exported. It then makes the person (`--me`, with its recovery code), a first agent sponsored by the person, and a settlement identity named `bank` (no recovery key) in the tenant's namespace (S100), claims the starter credits (S96) for the person and the agent, prints the recovery code once and, in a terminal, asks for its last six characters. `asp join` again says it is already joined; `asp leave` forgets the saved login only. The service's Google endpoints can be overridden for tests (`--google-auth-url`, `--google-token-url`, `--google-userinfo-url`). Tested end to end through a real service against a stand-in for Google: the address, the form's hidden challenge, the early pickup finding nothing, the page without a token, the whole setup, the saved login used by `account show` and `credits balance` with no export, joining again and leaving. Left: blocker 12 steps 3 and 4 (`asp run`, the install), a first agent has no runtime attached until `asp run`, the login file is plaintext, polling every two seconds. |
| S102 | `asp run <runtime>`: a governed session with no paperwork (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12, step 3) | `asp run claude-code [--prompt <text>] [--project <dir>] [--agent <name>] [--model <m>] [--budget n] [--minutes n] [--allow-network host,host]` uses the login and identities `asp join` saved. It makes a short job for the person and the agent behind the scenes (intent, offer, contract, a zero-value bond, a Mandate that allows `repo.read`, `repo.write` and `shell.exec` in the project, and no network unless hosts are named, with the default rate; spend cap 100, ending after 120 minutes by default), runs the tool through `asp gateway` (Claude Code on the user's own login, passed through to the Anthropic API, so no key is held), so a call the Mandate does not allow is removed before the tool receives it, settles the job as accepted when the tool stops (a kill by the gateway has already settled it), and prints the agent's own output as it comes and then a short summary: the job, the calls judged and refused with the scope and the reason, the tokens and where the signed evidence is. Without `--prompt` the tool runs interactively under the gateway. `asp run <package> --backend <runtime>` is unchanged. `asp join` now saves `identities {me, agent, bank}` in the login file; an older login falls back to the names in the namespace. Tested end to end through a real service with a stand-in for the Claude Code CLI and the model: not joined says to join, a call to read a file allowed and a curl refused (the agent never receives it), the job settled, the summary and no raw events. Left: other runtimes (Codex, OpenHands, Antigravity, Goose), memory and package write-back, the sandbox flag, the pre-call hook as a second layer, and each session costs about a dozen records against a tenant's quota (R1). |
| S103 | Session history, stop and standing jobs for `asp run` (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 items 1, 4 and 6; narrows R1) | **Standing job:** `asp run <runtime>` no longer makes a job for each session. The first run for an agent and a set of permissions makes a standing job (intent, offer, contract, zero-value bond, Mandate; `--standing-days`, default 7, the contract's deadline is the Mandate's expiry) and keeps it in `<ASP_HOME>/standing.json`; every later run reuses it (a different set of permissions, or `--allow-network` hosts, is a different job), so a session costs only its signed Action reports (tested: one to three records). A job with under fifteen minutes left, or no longer running, is settled (delivery, acceptance, settlement, all zero value) and replaced at the next run. `asp standing` lists the jobs and `asp standing end [<agent name>]` settles them. The intent's purpose carries a random suffix so two jobs made in the same second do not collide (CL1). **History:** each session has an eight-character id and two lines in `<ASP_HOME>/runs.ndjson` (start, end), private to the machine (the task text stays local); `asp runs [--all]` lists them newest first with their state (ended, stopped, failed, or `running?` when no end was recorded), calls and refusals, and `asp runs <id>` explains one: the task, the Mandate, the end, the refusals with their reasons, what the log holds for the standing job (records, signed Action reports, assurance, scopes used) and the run log's path. **Stop:** `asp stop [<id>]` leaves a stop file that the session's gateway sees (`asp gateway --stop-file`), refuses the agent's next request and now also ends the tool, after five seconds' grace to see the refusals and exit by itself (then SIGTERM, then SIGKILL five seconds later; ASP_GATEWAY_KILL_GRACE_MS); the standing job stays. The gateway ends the tool whenever it stops for any reason (the contract ended or was revoked, probing), not only for a stop. Left: items 2 and 3 and 5 of the blocker 12 list (inline approvals, `asp agent add`, other runtimes), headless runs, run-log custody; a standing job's Actions still count toward a tenant's quota (about one to three records a session). |
| S104 | Inline approvals for `asp run` (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 item 2) | `asp run claude-code --ask commands|writes|network|<scope>,...` puts a gate in the standing job's Mandate (`irreversible` policy checkpoint with those scopes, so the gate is signed and in the log; a different gate is a different standing job): a call on a gated scope is held by the gateway, which raises a Checkpoint signed by the agent (S83). With a prompt, at a terminal, the session asks you there (`Allow shell.exec: npm run build? [y/N]`) and records your signed answer (yes: approved; anything else: refused, with the reason in what the agent is told); without a terminal, or in an interactive session whose terminal belongs to the tool, it says to answer in another window. `asp approvals` lists what is waiting for you (the question and the proposed action, masked of secret-looking text) and `asp approvals approve|refuse [<n>]` answers one, signed with your key; both work from any window on the machine with the saved login. `--ask` must name a scope in the session's Mandate. Found and fixed on the way: the approval side opened the local log instead of the service's (`openLog(home)` without the environment), so a joined person's approvals would never have been seen; and gateway runs started in the same second shared one folder, so a decision file left by one run could answer a later run's request with the same id (an approval consumed by the wrong call); each run's folder now has a random part. Tested end to end through a real service: `--ask` naming a scope not in the Mandate refused, a held call answered from another window with `asp approvals` (it reaches the agent), asked at the terminal and answered yes (reaches the agent) and no (the agent is told, the call never arrives), the same standing job for all three, a different gate a different job, repeated four times for stability. Left: an inline prompt for interactive sessions, a Mandate gate chosen by the agent's own list of risky actions, approvals by mail or the dashboard for a standing job (U7). |
| S105 | `asp agent`: more than one agent (new, 2026-10; tooling and a service cap, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 item 3) | `asp agent add <name> [--purpose <text>]` makes another agent in the tenant's namespace (S100), sponsored by the person, with its own key on this machine (a person recovers it, S99); `asp agent list` shows each agent with whether its key is on this machine, how its key loss is recovered, how many sessions it has run and when last, its standing job's end, and which one is the default; `asp agent use <name>` makes one the default for `asp run` (saved in the login file); `asp run <runtime> --agent <name>` runs a named one. The service refuses a tenant's passport for a new agent past a cap (`asp serve --max-agents n`, default 20; an update to an existing agent, a person and an admin are not counted), so one sign-up cannot fill the log with identities. Tested through a real service: add, a taken name and a bad name refused, a named agent run and the history showing it, list, use, the default used by a run with no `--agent`, the cap (three here) refusing the fourth, and not joined saying to join. Left: retiring an agent (the protocol has no passport deletion, so an unused agent stays in the log), per-agent Mandates other than the runtime's defaults, a different runtime per agent. |
| S106 | `asp run codex` and `asp run goose` (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 item 5, narrows P21) | The session profile is now per runtime: `claude-code` (Anthropic API, the person's own Claude login passed through, a prompt optional), `codex` and `goose` (OpenAI-style APIs, a prompt required, Goose also a model). For these the person's own API key is named by an environment variable (`--provider openai|openrouter` picks the endpoint and the default variable, `OPENAI_API_KEY` or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`; `--api-key-env NAME` names another); the gateway holds it and removes it from the tool's environment (the tool gets a placeholder), so the provider sees the person's key and the tool never does. Codex runs through `codex-runtime.mjs` as a Responses-API provider pointed at the gateway (workspace-write sandbox, `-` leaves Codex's default model); Goose through `goose-runtime.mjs` as an OpenAI-compatible host with its developer extension. The same standing job, history, stop and approvals apply to every runtime (the history names the runtime). `asp run openhands` and `asp run antigravity` say they are not one-command sessions yet and give the pack-and-run form. Tested through a real service with stand-ins for the tools and the provider: a missing prompt, model, key or a bad provider named plainly, the real key reaching the provider and neither the tool nor the screen, a call refused before the tool received it, both sessions in the history under one standing job. Left (P21): OpenHands and Antigravity as one-command sessions (OpenHands needs its own server and Antigravity is governed by its hook rather than the gateway), the real Codex and Goose from this command (Codex through the gateway was checked live earlier, evals/gateway-codex.mjs; Goose by evals/gateway-agents.mjs), a ChatGPT login for Codex (P3). |
| S107 | Headless runs and machine tokens (new, 2026-10; tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 item 9) | `asp token create/list/revoke` makes named, expiring, revocable machine tokens (hash kept only; at most 10 active; 1 to 365 days) that can read the account and write records but not make tokens, claim credits, export or close it. `asp standing prepare` makes the standing job without running anything. `asp run <runtime> --headless --contract <id> --prompt/--prompt-file [--json] [--fail-on-refusal]` takes its login from ASP_LOG_URL/ASP_LOG_TOKEN, restores the agent's key from ASP_AGENT_BACKUP (ASP_BACKUP_PASSPHRASE) if absent, needs the person's own API key (held by the gateway), refuses --ask, and exits 10 (refusal with --fail-on-refusal) or 11 (job ended or Mandate within ten minutes of expiry). Composite action at .github/actions/asp-run; docs/ci.md. Tested end to end with a CI-like home. Left: a scheduled-task example beyond cron/Actions, real Anthropic API key run. |
| S108 | Provider-terms rule (new, 2026-10; documentation and terms, no protocol or code change; launch blocker 12 item 8; P22 now partial) | docs/provider-terms.md states the rule: the gateway is only the user's own local process; the service never sees a provider credential; hosted, headless and always-on agents use the user's own API key (headless already refuses without one, S107); a subscription login is for ordinary individual use at a terminal; no resold or pooled model access. Terms section 6 gains a "Model providers and your keys" paragraph (version stays 2026-10, as the beta has no accepted sign-ups yet; bump it before launch if any have). Not settled: Anthropic has not been asked about the exact setup; a lawyer has not read it. | Terms and docs only. |
| S109 | Run-log custody (new, 2026-10; a service route and client tooling, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 item 7; E16 now partial) | `asp serve --runlogs <dir>` keeps each tenant's sealed run logs (`PUT/GET/DELETE /runlogs/<id>`, `GET /runlogs`; per-tenant folder, 64 MiB quota, 16 MiB each; the service checks the envelope's shape and nothing else, it holds no key). `asp run-log keep` seals a log on the person's machine (AES-256-GCM under a random key, wrapped by X25519 to each of the identity's live keys, the signing key and the recovery key; the X25519 key is derived from the Ed25519 seed, so no new secret exists), `kept`, `fetch`, `drop`, `open` (decrypts, checks the hash chain and the sealed head and any disclosure signatures). `asp run-log disclose --to <did,..> --reason <why> [--upload]` wraps the same key to the named people and adds an owner-signed statement (log hash, recipients, reason, time) without re-encrypting; a disclosed copy cannot be replaced by a different log. `asp run <runtime> --keep-run-log` seals the session's log at the end. Account export includes `runlogs/`; account close deletes them; the privacy notice lists them; the setup script creates and backs up the folder. Not built: encryption of the local file (E6), a default or reminder, headless sealing, rules for a refused disclosure, a live check. | packages/asp-package/test/runlog-vault.test.ts, packages/asp-cli/test/runlog-custody.test.ts; docs/run-log-custody.md |
| S110 | Discoverability (new, 2026-10; tooling and a public help page, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 item 10; U16 new, partial) | `asp` with no arguments is a short home that reads only local files: before joining, one step (`asp join`); after, the everyday commands and the next step given the state (nothing run yet, a session running, a standing job ending within a day). The full reference moves to `asp --help` and `asp help all`. `asp status` adds the service (reachable, key-recovery warnings), running sessions, the last session, the approvals waiting and the standing jobs, and says so when the service cannot be reached. `asp help [topic|words]` is a registry of 16 topics shared with the service. `asp ask "<words>"` follows fixed rules (no model): it shows the command, asks `Run it? [y/N]` (or runs with `--yes`; with no terminal and no `--yes` it only shows), never runs a command that cannot be undone (account close, standing end), and says when a command still has a placeholder or when it did not understand. `asp init` writes asp.yaml; `asp run` with no runtime and a project asp.yaml starts the runtime it names, flags win over the file, a typo is an error naming the line, and `allow-network` is used only from a file whose hash you trusted (`asp trust`, or `asp init` which wrote it), so a cloned repository cannot grant itself the network; headless runs ignore asp.yaml. `asp shim bash|zsh|powershell` prints completion and a `claude` function that starts `asp run claude-code` (no arguments, or `-p "..."`) and passes anything else to the real claude with a notice; asp never edits startup files. The service serves a public, script-free, searchable `/help` and `/help/<topic>`; the pages after sign-up show the everyday lines. | packages/asp-package/test/guide.test.ts, packages/asp-cli/test/discover.test.ts |
| S111 | Per-command approval (new, 2026-10; gateway and CLI, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 / gap U15) | A Mandate gate holds every call on a scope. Now the person can narrow which shell commands they are asked about: `asp run <runtime> --ask risky` (a built-in list in gateway/commands.ts: git push, reset --hard, clean, rm with -r, sudo, publish commands, docker push/rm, kubectl delete/apply, terraform apply/destroy, ssh/scp, curl or wget piped to a shell, drop table, delete without where, and others), or `--ask-match <words|re:regex>` (repeatable; words match as whole words anywhere in the text, also inside `bash -c` or after `&&`; `re:` is a case-insensitive regular expression; asp.yaml `ask-match`); `--ask commands` still asks about every command. At the prompt `a` means yes and never ask about that exact command again for that agent (saved in `<ASP_HOME>/always-allow.json`; offered only for a command shown whole, not cut short or masked); `asp approvals always` lists them and `asp approvals forget <n|all>` takes them back. The signed gate in the Mandate stays the ceiling; patterns decide which gated calls the person is asked about (the person is the principal of their own session), and the run log marks each shell call as `gated` or `gate_skipped: remembered|no-match`. The prompt now reads `[y/N/a]` where `a` is offered. | packages/asp-package/test/commands.test.ts, packages/asp-cli/test/run-ask-match.test.ts, run-approvals.test.ts |
| S112 | Antigravity and OpenHands sessions (new, 2026-10; CLI only, no protocol change; launch blocker 12 / gap P21) | `asp run antigravity --prompt "..."` and `asp run openhands --prompt "..." [--model m --endpoint url --api-key-env NAME]` are governed sessions like the others: the same login, identities, standing job (shared with the agent's other runtimes when the permissions are the same), history, summary and `--ask` prompts. They differ underneath: there is no gateway, so each session makes a temporary package (`asp pack`) and runs it on the package path (`asp run <package> --backend <runtime> --contract <standing job> --no-write-back --no-canary`): the runtime's own pre-call hook blocks a call outside the Mandate before it runs, its output is observed, the signed Action is reported (assurance hook_enforced for Antigravity, runtime_observed for OpenHands), and a violation ends the run and settles the standing job as the package path does. The tool's model calls are not judged (P2). `asp stop` ends the tool (a stop-file watcher in the package path; the standing job stays). `--ask risky|--ask-match` are not applied (every command is asked about, with a note); headless is refused. The temporary package is removed after the session. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-hooked.test.ts |
| S113 | `asp join` survives a refused starter claim and shows the recovery code first (new, 2026-10-12; CLI fix found in the first live run; closes P23) | The recovery code is printed as soon as the person identity exists (before the agent and bank identities and the credits step); the login file is saved with the identities as soon as they are made; a starter-credit refusal (for example "this Google account has already claimed its starter credits") is shown on the credits line as `none (<reason>)` and no longer stops the join. | packages/asp-cli/test/join.test.ts (second tenant by the same Google account) |
| S114 | Each session records its own run log; `asp run --keep-run-log` works (new, 2026-10-12; CLI fixes found in the first live run; closes P24) | The session end record in `runs.ndjson` now carries `runLog` (read from the gateway or package run output); `asp runs <id>` shows that log (an older session falls back to the job's newest); the session summary prints the log's path, or with `--keep-run-log` seals it to the service at once (S109); a service started without `--runlogs` is reported as such instead of a route error. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-ask-match.test.ts |
| S115 | Test runs are told from other shell commands properly (new, 2026-10-12; classifier fix found in the live approval test; closes P25) | `deriveScopeForTool` (and its copies in the two pre-call hook scripts, kept equal by a test) returns `tests.run` only when every part of the command (split on `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, newlines) is a known test runner (npm/pnpm/yarn/bun test, pytest, python -m pytest/unittest, jest, vitest, mocha, cargo test, go test, make test, mvn/gradle test, dotnet test, phpunit, rspec, ctest, node --test, deno test, tox) or a `cd`; before, any command containing the letters `test` was a test run. Sessions now grant `tests.run` with the shell (`repo.read, repo.write, shell.exec, tests.run`); `--ask commands` and `--ask-match` gate both; the per-command filter and the `a` answer apply to both. Standing jobs made earlier have the older three scopes and are replaced by new ones on the next run. | packages/asp-package/test/test-run-scope.test.ts |
| S116 | `asp join` brings the person back to the terminal (new, 2026-10-12; CLI, from the first live run; narrows U16) | A browser page cannot focus another application, so the command does: when the sign-up arrives it prints `>>> Signed up as <tenant>. You are back in the terminal`, sets the window title, rings the bell, and runs the operating system's own way to raise the window and show a notification (Windows: AppActivate on the title and a balloon tip; macOS: a notification and `activate` for Terminal or iTerm; Linux: notify-send), best effort, only at a terminal, off with ASP_NO_FOCUS. The commands come from a pure function (`returnToTerminalPlan`) that a test reads. Inside an embedded terminal panel (such as the Claude app's) only the banner, bell and notification can work. | packages/asp-cli/test/discover.test.ts |
| S117 | Fewer Action records per session (new, 2026-10-12; gateway reporting rule, found in the long-horizon run; narrows R1) | The gateway reported an Action about 2 s after any call (S73, so a revoke could not lose activity), which wrote about one record per tool call. Now an Action goes out at once only for a blocked attempt (as before) or soon after a scope that this session has not yet reported; calls on scopes already reported wait for the 30 s interval or the exit report. The late-report rules (S80, S81) and the E15 guarantees for new information are unchanged; repeated activity on an already-reported scope can now wait up to 30 s, and the late report covers it if the job ends first. | packages/asp-cli/test/gateway.test.ts (E15 cases) |
| S118 | An approval that timed out no longer lingers (new, 2026-10-12; CLI fix found in the long-horizon run; closes P28) | The approval server now counts the wait from the request's `requested_at` (written by the gateway and both pre-call hooks) instead of from when its Checkpoint was raised: a request that timed out while queued behind an earlier approval is refused at once, without a Checkpoint, and a raised Checkpoint expires when the gateway gives up. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-approvals.test.ts, checkpoint and gate tests (regression; the queued-retry case itself was seen live and is not yet a dedicated test) |
| S119 | The gateway stops asking after unanswered approvals (new, 2026-10-12; gateway rule found in the long-horizon run; narrows P29) | After two approval requests in one gateway session time out unanswered, the gateway no longer holds gated calls for an answer: it refuses them at once (no strike) with the reason `<scope> needs the principal's approval, and N earlier approval request(s) in this session went unanswered, so no more are asked: continue without this call, or stop and tell the person`. The limit is 2, or `ASP_MAX_UNANSWERED_APPROVALS`. An approval that is answered, in either direction, does not count. | packages/asp-package/test/gateway.test.ts |
| S120 | An expired Checkpoint is closed by whoever looks next (new, 2026-10-12; CLI fix found in the long-horizon run; closes P30) | `asp approvals`, `asp status` (through the same listing) and the approval server of a new session now sign the expiry of a Checkpoint whose time has run out and whose session is gone, as the performer, so a job is not left in Checkpoint after `asp stop` or a crash. Also: `asp` checks the Node version first and says plainly what to install (T1), and a session whose tool could not start says which tool and how to install it. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-approvals.test.ts, packages/asp-cli/test/node-check.test.ts |
| S121 | One approval request per command at a time (new, 2026-10-12; gateway fix found by dissecting the long-horizon run; closes P31) | A gated call that is the same as one already waiting for the principal's answer (same scope and command) joins that request instead of making another, and gets its answer; the count behind the unanswered-approvals limit (S119) counts requests, not callers. The Claude Code pre-call hook path is not changed. Also: docs/commands.md, a catalogue of every asp command with a one-line description (generated, with a test that fails when the CLI has a command the catalogue lacks), and evals/long-horizon-report.mjs, a script that dissects a long-horizon run (liveness, cost, governance, integrity, outcome). | packages/asp-package/test/gateway.test.ts, packages/asp-cli/test/commands-doc.test.ts |
| S122 | `asp run --local` and plain connection errors (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows T4) | `asp run <runtime> --local --prompt ...` runs the same governed session with no service and no account: the first use makes a person, an agent and a settlement identity as did:key in `<ASP_HOME>/local`, the standing job and every record are in a local log there, and nothing is sent anywhere; the hosted login is not read or changed. Not available with `--agent` or `--headless`. Any command that fails on the network now says `cannot reach the service at <url> (<reason>)` with what still works, instead of Node's bare `fetch failed`. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-local.test.ts |
| S123 | Ctrl-C and a closed terminal end a session cleanly (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows T2) | Found by the live Ctrl-C test: interrupting `asp run` left the session `running?` with no end record for ever, and the command the agent was running (a script started by Claude Code) kept running. Now the first Ctrl-C (or SIGTERM or SIGHUP) writes the session's stop file, so the session ends as `asp stop` does and is recorded `stopped`; a second Ctrl-C leaves at once and still records the end; the agent is ended with its whole process tree on Windows (`taskkill /T`); `asp runs` shows a session whose process has gone without an end record as `interrupted`. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-interrupt.test.ts |
| S124 | Ctrl-C at an approval prompt ends the session and closes the held call (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows T2) | Found live: at the inline prompt Ctrl-C was read as a plain "no", so the session carried on and finished normally; closing the prompt on its own left the question unsettled so the process fell out with no end record, the Checkpoint open and a stop file behind. Now Ctrl-C at the prompt means leave: the session is stopped, the held call is refused to the agent, and the Checkpoint is resolved by the principal's signed refusal (an expiry is not allowed before its time); a closed prompt settles as no. `asp stop` closes a held Checkpoint the same way. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-interrupt-approval.test.ts |
| S125 | An interactive session leaves Ctrl-C to the tool (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows T2) | In a session with no prompt the tool owns the terminal and uses Ctrl-C for itself (cancel an answer, press twice to leave), so `asp` no longer ends the session on SIGINT there: the session ends when the tool does. A closed terminal (SIGHUP) or SIGTERM still stops it, and the handlers are per signal. Prompted sessions are unchanged. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-interrupt.test.ts |
| S126 | Two terminals on one agent (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows T3) | Found by running two sessions of one agent at once: (1) the gateway watcher read the job's state as unknown while the other session was writing the log and took that for the job ending, so a session was stopped with `the contract is now undefined`; an unknown state is no longer an end. (2) A second session's approval waits behind the first (a job has one open Checkpoint) and is shown once the first is answered. (3) Waiting for an approval re-read the job's whole chain every second and, with two terminals, ran into the service's request limit, which was then taken for a refusal; now only the job's state is polled while waiting, the expired-approval check runs every 15 seconds, and a rate limit is waited out. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-concurrent.test.ts |
| S127 | New tenants start at 600 requests a minute (new, 2026-10-13; log service default, no protocol change; narrows T12) | Two terminals on one agent stalled at the starting limit of 120 requests a minute. The starting request limit is 600; the write limit stays 120 a minute, the records and bytes quotas are unchanged, and tenants signed up earlier keep their stored limit until an operator raises it. | packages/asp-log/test/signup.test.ts |
| S128 | Interactive approvals say where to answer (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows U15) | Found in the owner's first interactive session: asp cannot ask in a window the tool owns, so a held command printed one line inside the tool's screen, and the owner typed y into the tool's own input box, which answered nothing. Now an interactive session with a gate says before the tool starts that approvals are answered in a second terminal (`asp approvals approve` or `refuse`) and that typing in the tool's box does not answer them, and the line printed when a command is held says the same. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-interrupt.test.ts |
| S129 | A model provider's refusal or outage is named, and a refused key stops the run (new, 2026-10-13; CLI and gateway only, no protocol change; narrows T2) | Found live with a wrong key against the real Anthropic API: Claude Code retried in silence (nine 401s in two minutes) and the person saw nothing. Now the gateway reports a provider's error status or an unreachable provider (never the provider's own text, which can echo a key): `provider <host> refused the API key (HTTP 401): ...` live and in the session summary, once per kind, in words for 401/403, 402, 404, 429, 5xx, other 4xx and no connection; and after three refusals of the key in a row the run is stopped, since retrying a refused key cannot succeed. The run log records each as `model_error`. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-provider-errors.test.ts |
| S130 | The network drops mid-session (new, 2026-10-13; CLI and gateway only, no protocol change; narrows T2) | Three findings from taking the service away, and the model provider's connection, in the middle of a session: (1) a call held for approval was refused the moment the service could not be reached, instead of waiting for it to come back; the approval wait now retries a connection failure as it does a rate limit. (2) Every command that reaches the service said only `fetch failed`; now `cannot reach the log service at <url> (nothing is answering there: the service may be down)`. (3) When the service is out of reach at the end of a session only the record cannot be sent: the session now reads `finished` (not `failed (exit 1)`), says `record   NOT recorded in the log` and `this session's signed Action is not in the log`, and names the run log on this machine as the record, where it used to say the Action reports were in the log. A provider that cuts the connection mid-answer is named (`lost the connection to <host> while it was answering`) and the agent gets a clear 502 instead of half an answer. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-network-drop.test.ts |
| S131 | `asp join` survives the browser step going wrong (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; narrows P23) | The wait for the sign-up (the one place a person leaves the terminal) used to end the whole join if the connection dropped for one poll, and said only `timed out`. Now a dropped connection is said once and the wait goes on; a service that is down at the start is named with its address; with `--no-open` (or no browser: SSH, a container) the printed address says it can be opened on any other device and the terminal picks up the result; the wait runs out with what to do, including that a sign-up finished too late exists but its login never reached this terminal, so a different name is needed. `ASP_JOIN_TIMEOUT_MS` shortens the wait for tests. | packages/asp-cli/test/join-edges.test.ts |
| S132 | A session that cannot be recorded is still a success, and its report is kept (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; closes T16) | Found by filling a tenant's record quota: the sessions kept running (the Mandate is enforced on this machine and needs no credits or quota), but each said only `the service refused it`, exited 1 although the agent succeeded, and kept nothing to send later. Now: the summary says why (`your account's record limit is full`, `the service could not be reached`) and that the run log is complete; the exit code is 0 when only the record failed; the unsent report waits in `<ASP_HOME>/outbox` and is sent by the next session by itself (a service that does not answer is not waited for beyond 8 seconds; a report that can never be accepted, such as one for a settled job, is dropped); `asp status` shows `N of M records` with a heads-up from 90% and how many reports wait. | packages/asp-cli/test/run-quota-full.test.ts |
| S133 | An accepted job pays by default (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; found by the credit-ledger rehearsal) | `asp market settle --basis accepted|silence` with no amounts used to release 0 of the escrow, so the performer was paid nothing and the escrow went back to the payer; it now releases the escrow's agreed price (less any `--fees`) and returns the bond that was not slashed, as a ruling already derived its amounts. Also new: `packages/asp-cli/src/ledger.ts` and `evals/credit-ledger.mjs`, which show where every credit is by entity (balances, stakes, escrow, bond, the protocol's court-fee reserve) and check that the total adds up, with the tokens of the sessions as an estimate in credits that pays no provider. | packages/asp-cli/test/credit-ledger.test.ts |
| S134 | Under a contract, look-only shell commands are reads (new, 2026-10-13; the Claude Code hook and the shared classifier, no protocol change; found in the first live job) | The pre-call hook and `deriveScopeForTool` (used for the Action report) classed every shell command as `shell.exec`, so under a Mandate that granted only `repo.read` Claude Code's `ls -la` was blocked before it ran and counted as strike 1 of 3: three of those would have stopped the job and marked the agent. The gateway's judge already read a look-only command as a read. Now a command whose every part only looks (ls, cat, grep, find without -delete or -exec, git status, and so on, joined by cd, pipes or &&) is `repo.read` in both copies of the classifier; anything that writes, runs something, substitutes a command or fetches is not. With `shell.exec` gated, a shell call still waits for the principal whatever it is classed as, as under the gateway. The run log's last line for a run under a contract no longer reads `undefined request(s)`. | packages/asp-package/test/mandate-hook.test.ts |
| S135 | `asp credits`: credits on request only (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change) | `asp credits` with no arguments lists the saved login's person, agent and bank, and what is locked in escrow and bond in the standing jobs and in the jobs this home made (`<ASP_HOME>/jobs.json`, written by `asp market contract`). A running session mentions nothing about credits while it runs. | packages/asp-cli/test/credit-ledger.test.ts |
| S136 | Credits in every summary (new, 2026-10-13; CLI only, no protocol change; supersedes S135's rule that credits are shown only on request) | The owner chose that credits appear in every session's summary. | S137 | A node key signs for a contract only under that contract's Mandate (new, 2026-10-14; a log rule, no schema change; narrows T22, items 1 and 2) | A node key (a sub-agent's short-lived key) that signs a record naming a contract is refused unless it was granted citing a Mandate (`node_no_mandate`) and that Mandate is for that same contract (`node_wrong_contract`). The Python SDK does not implement the log, so there are no new vectors: the rule is in the log service and its tests. | S138 | One public key is one node key, and a node at the limit can still be revoked (new, 2026-10-14; log rules, no schema change; narrows T22) | A node grant whose public key is already registered under the same DID (as another node, or as one of the person's own passport keys) is refused (`node_key_reused`). Grants under no Mandate are still not capped in number by the log (the tenant's record quota and rate limit are the only limits); none of those keys can sign for a contract (S137). A label for a reader; it grants nothing, and helpers run as protocol nodes (`asp orchestrate`) have keys of their own and are not marked. `asp run claude-code`, `asp run <package> --backend claude-code` and `asp market action --sub-agents shared_identity` set it; other runtimes are not marked because it is not known whether they run helpers. Every accepted act is appended to one hash chain (`/forum/head`) that `/forum/verify` re-walks; a `hide` needs an admin tenant and a public reason. Reads (`/forum/feed`, `/forum/posts/<id>`, `/forum/citizens/<did>` with tier, slashes and accepted jobs from the log, plain-text front door, `/forum/llms.txt`, escaped HTML pages) are public; writes need a tenant token. A handle is 3 to 32 characters (a lowercase letter, then lowercase letters, digits or `-`), unique, with a reserved list. Registration is throttled to 3 per address and 300 per service per hour; the address is hashed under a random salt, and the hashes of the last hour are kept in `joins.json` beside the forum so a restart does not reset the limit. A wrong passphrase or a changed file is refused with a plain message; a sealed file without the passphrase says how to set it. An address is stored only as the first 32 hex characters of a salted SHA-256; at most 5000 records are kept, newest first, and expired ones are dropped on each write. It is written only when a sign-in fails. A damaged or unwritable file is ignored (the lockout still holds in memory). The request buckets (per tenant, per write, per address) are shared the same way: each process publishes its cumulative use in `<file>.buckets` about every 500 ms and deducts the others' increase from its own bucket; a process silent for 10 seconds is forgotten; the file names an address only by its salted hash. The Settlement gains the basis `ruling_timeout`. New lifecycle effect `mark_disputed` (the rejection records when the dispute opened) and guard `past_ruling_window` (the settlement's `issued_at` is later than the dispute's plus the window). New transition: from `Disputed`, a Settlement with basis `ruling_timeout`, issued by the bank, guards `refs_contract`, `no_ruling_yet`, `past_ruling_window`. The log requires the amounts: `escrow_released` is half the escrow locked (rounded down), `bond_slashed` and `fees` are 0 (rule `settlement_mismatches_timeout`). With no window on the Contract, a timeout settlement is refused: a dispute waits for its ruling as before. Four conformance vectors: `ruling_timeout_after_window`, `ruling_timeout_before_window`, `ruling_timeout_without_window`, `ruling_timeout_after_ruling`.
